| `Armour.moveShell`, `hitFirst`, `legacyHit`, `blastK`, `moduleDown` | combat.js `moveShot`, `explode`, `damage`, `destroyPart` | Shells, plated craft and module parts (chapter 8 §6) |
| `Death.airPart`, `afterAir`, `groundPart`, `detonated`, `groundPieces` | combat.js `destroyPart`, `flushRips` | The big update's damage model (js/death.js, §1.5): what 0 hp does to an aircraft's part (the air rule), then crippled or down; an old car's cab (its driver knocked out); a ground vehicle's torn-off kit tagged as junk |
| `Death.base`, `hit`, `ignite`, `partDead` | combat.js `damage` | An aircraft's state when first hit; our hit's HUD tick (not a fire's damage); the 30% fire roll (a hit only, never a crash); a dead engine or knocked-out cockpit takes nothing more |
| `Death.tick(F, e, n)`, `Death.partFX` | combat.js `frame`'s 0.07 s smoke tick | Fires, falling hulks, rest and age, crash sites, junk, ground hulks settling into wrecks and the wrecks' fire and smoke; a part smoked its own way (an aircraft's damage trail; a knocked-out vehicle's parts: none, its wreck's smoke is `wreckFX`'s) |
| `Death.impact(v, parts, sim)` | flight.js `breakParts` (first line, cause `'impact'`) | A crash meets the damage model first: an aircraft hulk (or any aircraft whose cockpit breaks) goes up in one fireball, a ground vehicle whose hull or cab breaks is knocked out and frozen where it lies; a rocket's, and a plane's gear on landing, as ever |
| `Death.drawHits`, `Death.reset`, `Death.lose`/`holdLost`/`flushLost` | combat.js `drawHUD`, `reset`, `damage`, `holdRips`/`flushRips` | The air HUD's hit tick and banner; a new flight clears the banner and the crash sites (a respawn keeps them); the player's losses of one hit window said in one toast |
| `Radar.step`, `sees`, `mainRadar`, `guideRange`, `lockTime`, `busy`, `heard`, `call`, `draw`, `reset` | combat.js `frame`, `guideAir`, `playerFrame`, `launchMissile`, `rangeOf`, `drawRWR`, `reset` | The big update's radar parts (js/radar.js, chapter 9 §0 has the whole table): the player's picture, solo locks on us, what a radar holds, locks and guidance, the scope |
| `Drone.frame`, `step`, `cookOff`, `detonate`, `threats`, `alarm`, `netFire` | combat.js `frame`, `step`, `destroyPart` (slot 3), `playerFrame`, `candidates`, `damage`, `netGuns`, all behind `Combat.S.dr` or a drone field | The big update's drones and counter-drone kit (js/drone.js, chapter 10 §0 has the whole table): links, bays, fuses, the anti-drone gun and laser, marks; `S.dr` is false for every old flight |
| `Flight.onDestroyed`, `afterFall`, `endNow`, `strip`, `deathLine` | death.js (`airCheck`, `crash`, `groundKO`), `Armour.knockOut`; mp.js's and groundai.js's end timers | No end screen over the fall: the strip, then the end screen 1.2 s after the crash (at most 10 s after the death) |

Remote craft are skipped by physics (flight.js:524). They only coast along their velocity on each substep (flight.js:639). The ground AI's vehicles (`v.kin`) and wrecks (`v.wreck`) are skipped too (flight.js:612).

---

### 1. combat.js

**Mode.** `mode()` (combat.js:28) reads `Settings.get('targets')`, which is `practice`, `hostile` or `off`. It is forced to `'off'` whenever `MP.active` (combat.js:27), so a multiplayer room never has range targets, SAMs or AI fighters. Any new AI or target code must check `mpOn()` too. The same setting drives the Tank Range on Highland Ridge (PR #38, groundai.js, chapter 8 §12), which a room never has either.

#### 1.1 Weapon parts (parts.js:349-420)

`weapon.ammo` counts **both copies** of a twin pair. `each` is the kg released per round: `p.store = ammo*each`, and the mass is recomputed when `each >= 10`.

Related defs:
- `radarSet` (the big update): the nine radar parts' numbers (chapter 9 §1). Before it, `radar` reach in metres sat on twelve cockpits and noses, the Radome, the Drone Core and the Rotodome (cockpitJet 12000, cockpitTandem 10000, cockpitStealth 14000, droneCore 6000, radome 16000, rotodome 45000; the jets' noses 8000-28000); those numbers are gone, and a nose that had one names the set Fit a radar puts back (`radarFit`).
- `armor`: fuselageArmor 0.6.
- `stealth`: cockpitStealth and fuselageStealth 0.6, bombBay 0.5. The 5th-generation jets' parts (PR #36): the FS-22's 0.7 (its engines 0.6), the FS-35's 0.65 (engine 0.6), the Sugoi-57's hull, bays, booms and fins 0.6.
- `irst` (PR #36): the IRST Ball (`ruIRST`, parts.js:503) 0.3. `irK`: `us5Eng22` 0.7, `us5Eng35` 0.85.
- `rwr` (PR #37): 1 on `rwr`, 2 on `rwrPlus`. `rwrOf(v)` (combat.js:177) is the best on board, 0 for none; the warning (§1.4) and the HUD (§1.7) read only that number, so a receiver shot off leaves the craft deaf.
- `retired` (PR #37): only on `turret`. `partFamilies` (builder.js:309) and the tier list of `App.showResearch` (main.js:337) leave the part out; everything else (loading, joining, flying, fighting, multiplayer spawn rows) treats it as any part. Retiring is how a part leaves the tray without breaking old designs (the key must stay, CLAUDE.md).
- Optional air-to-air overrides read by `airframe()`: `drag`, `bleed`, `gimbal`, `track`, `arm`, `irccm`.

#### 1.2 Weapon systems and firing

- **`systems(v)`** (combat.js:158) groups weapon parts by `p.type`, so all guns of one type fire together. Flares, chaff and turrets are excluded (they work by themselves). The result is cached on `v._ws`, keyed by `parts.length`. `v.wsel` is the selected index, and `cycle()` (combat.js:1429) changes it (Tab).
- **`status(v)`** (combat.js:1438) is what the weapon box shows: the chosen system's name (`w.label`, else `SHORT` by kind, :1452: GUN, RKT, AAM, RADAR, AGM, BOMB; the radar missile's label was AMRAAM until PR #36's naming rule, and the jets' missiles carry labels of their own, RV-77, RV-74, RV-13), rounds and count, the lock, kills, `flares` and `chaff` left and `stealth`; since the big update, for an air radar missile, `radar` (`Radar.chipState`: `ok`, `off`, `none`, `guns`, `out`; anything but `ok` puts ⊘ before the name, chapter 9 §8). With no system it returns null, unless the craft carries a turret (`TURRET`, its rounds) or flares or chaff (`FLARES`, `CHAFF` or `CM`, `ammo: null`, `cmOnly: true`, and `fl`/`ch` saying which it has, so an emptied one still shows `✦0`/`≋0`). The HUD side is in chapter 2 §4.
- **Trigger** (`step` combat.js:474, `playerFrame` combat.js:397):
  - Guns fire while the trigger is held. Each part keeps its own `p.cool`.
  - Rockets ripple one per `1/rate` through `nextStore`.
  - Missiles and bombs release once per trigger press (rising edge, or `F.fireTap`).
- **`zAt(p, side)`** (combat.js:194): a twin copy sits at `side * partZ(p)`; a single part sits at `partZ(p)`. Twin `p.z` is the spread, always ≥ 0 (parts.js:899-908).
- **`muzzle(p, side)`** (combat.js:196) gives the release point in part space:
  - a `bayRack` part (PR #36; :198): its own slot, `bayRack[ammo − 1]` (`[x, y, z]` in the part's frame, z added to the copy's), the store the 3D model hides next, so each missile leaves from where it was seen (the FS-22's main bay fires from z 0.78, −0.78, 0.47, −0.47, 0.16 and −0.16 in turn);
  - bay: below the belly at `w*0.6`, alternating ±0.6 by ammo parity;
  - stores and pods: offset `w*0.12`;
  - guns: the tip (`h/2`).
- **`nextSide(p)`** (combat.js:208):
  - a single part fires from the side it sits on (`sideOf(partZ)`);
  - twin rockets alternate `-(p.side||1)`;
  - twin missiles and bombs go right on even ammo and left on odd. This must match the store-visibility formula in parts3d.js:1805-1808.
- **`nextStore(sys)`** (combat.js:212) chooses among parts with ammo. It prefers a part whose next side differs from `sys.last`, then the smallest `relN` (the rack that has waited longest). `release()` (combat.js:233) decrements ammo and updates `p.store`.
- **`fireGun`** (combat.js:242):
  - Twin guns alternate `p.side` every round.
  - **Convergence:** a muzzle with `|z| > 0.3` is toed in by `cz = -m.z/600`, so the rounds meet 600 m ahead. That is where the HUD pipper sits (combat.js:1252).
  - Random spread on each axis; rounds live 3 s; a tracer every 3rd round (every round for exploding shells).
  - With `net=true` it fires show rounds: `dmg: 0`, and `s.net` set so they skip hit tests.
- **Doors** (PR #36; combat.js:219-231), for parts with `def.doors` (the FS-22/35 bays and guns, the Sugoi-57's bays): they snap open to fire and shut again a moment after the last shot.
  - `openDoors(p)` (:223) sets `p.doorT` to `DOOR_HOLD[kind]` (:222: gun 0.35 s, missile 1.4 s, bomb 1.8 s). `release()` calls it for every store, and `fireGun` for our own rounds (:257, not `net` ones).
  - `doorStep(v, h)` (:224) runs from `step` with the physics for our own craft only (`F.active`, not `v.remote`, :476): `p.deployed` opens in 0.2 s while `doorT` lasts and shuts in 0.4 s. The door parts are found once per part count (`v._doors`).
  - `p.deployed` is what the 2D art, the 3D `hinge` doors and `stealthOf` read, and it already travels in multiplayer part states (`pv`), so another player sees the doors open and the stealth drop. A craft you switch away from within the hold keeps its doors open until you come back.
- **`fireRocket`** (combat.js:265): starts at `w.speed`, motor from t=0. **`launchMissile`** (combat.js:277): dropped at +4 m/s "down" (a plane's part +x), no motor and no guidance for the first 0.35 s. **`dropBomb`** (combat.js:290): +2 m/s down. In multiplayer, rockets and bombs call `MP.sendShot` and missiles call `MP.sendMissile`.
- **`turretStep`** (combat.js:355), for every turret on our own craft (called from `step`, :493; `v.turrets = 0` stops the search on a craft without one):
  - aims at the nearest air candidate within `range`, leading it;
  - slews at 2.5 rad/s;
  - fires once within 0.06 rad;
  - **only in the air** (PR #37): `grounded` is `v.contactTime > 0` (anything touching ground or water, vessel.js:1058) or the craft held or being thrown by a catapult (`F.catapult` with `C.v === v` and `state !== 'done'`). Then it looks for nothing, slews the guns back to yaw 0, elevation 0 and holds its fire. Before this a field of turrets round a parked plane, or on a car, shot down anything that came near. The old small turret obeys it too;
  - `p.aimYaw`, `p.aimEl` and `p.gunOn` are replicated through `pv`.

#### 1.3 Projectiles (`moveShot` combat.js:519)

A shot is a plain object with these fields:
- `k`: `bullet`, `rocket`, `missile`, `bomb` or `torp`, and since PR #38 `shell` (a ground-update gun's round, with `sh` its SHELLS key: `moveShot` hands it to `Armour.moveShell`, chapter 8 §6)
- position and velocity, plus `dx/dy/dz` heading
- `t`, `life`, `dmg`, `blast`, `owner`, `enemy`
- `net`: the owning pid, on a replica
- `netId`: missile replicas only

Step order:
1. Gravity and drag (`k·ρ·v` with k = 1.2e-4 for bullets, 3e-5 for bombs, 6e-5 otherwise; air missiles use `s.drag`).
2. Segment-vs-part-box test against every vessel on the same body (`hitVessel` combat.js:106). The owner is skipped for the first 1.5 s. Replicas test nothing.
3. Range targets.
4. An unarmed hit (`t < s.arm`) is a dud that does 10% damage.
5. Missile proximity fuse at `blast*0.45`.
6. The props that stop shells (chapter 8 §14), nearer than the craft or target: `Props.shot` (the ground update's own rockets and missiles: `Props.shell`); `propShot` bursts a blast there (a replica's look only), a plain round puffs stone, wood, earth or leaves, or sparks off metal.
7. Direct hit: `explode()` if the shot has blast, else `damage()`.
8. Ground or sea: explode, or splash.

Other fates:
- A missile that times out self-destructs: `blast*0.4`, dmg 0.
- A cluster bomb bursts 150 m above the ground after 1 s (`burst` combat.js:614).
- A torpedo hitting water at ≤110 m/s becomes `k:'torp'` (`swim` combat.js:626). It runs at 30 m/s, 1.2 m under the surface, homing on the nearest **range sea target** within 4 km. There are no such targets in multiplayer, so torpedoes cannot hurt players.

Part hit boxes (`boxesOf` combat.js:82):
- every part: its `partRect`;
- across (z): a twin copy is ±`max(w,0.6)/2`, a wheel ±`track/2+0.3`, anything else ±`partDepth/2`; a wing with half shot off (`p.cut`, the big update's damage model), and the half that fell, only the half that is there;
- one box per entry of `partZs(p)`.

`hitVessel` returns early when the segment's closest approach to the vessel is more than `reach+2` away.

#### 1.4 Missile guidance and how to beat it

**Lock** (combat.js:406-426):
- Candidates come from `candidates(v, seek)` (combat.js:454):
  - for `air`: AI bandits, air drones, and enemy ghosts that are not landed and not cars;
  - for ground seekers: ground targets, plus enemy ghosts that are landed or are cars. The `radar` seeker (ARM) takes emitter targets only, never players.
  - Ghosts flagged `hiddenUntilSnap` are excluded.
- Minimum range: 350 m air, 150 m ground. The candidate must be inside `w.cone`.
- Range comes from `rangeOf` (combat.js:282): radar missiles get `min(range, Radar.guideRange(v))`, the reach of the craft's main radar part, so **none at all without a radar that guides** (the big update: there was a 5 km fallback, and cockpits and noses carried a hidden radar). A heat seeker (`seek: 'air'`, not radar) gets `range·(1 + irstOf(v))` (PR #36): `irstOf` (:174) is the best `def.irst` on the craft, so an IRST Ball (0.3) makes the stock heat seekers lock from 6.5 km instead of 5. Radar and ground missiles are unchanged (`combat.test.js` checks).
- IR range is further scaled by `clamp(√irSig, 0.2, 1)`, so the cold-target and head-on cuts scale with the IRST too.
- SARH needs `Radar.sees(v, t, tp, 'guide') === true` (the radar part's cone, reach, look-down, notch gate and line of sight: chapter 9 §4), and holds its lock while the radar holds the target unless `chaffBreaks` (combat.js:441) fires; a held lock lost says LOST on the scope. The target the player picked (`Radar.S.pick`: I, a tap) wins over the nearest the nose inside the seeker's cone.
- Lock time is 0.9 s for IR (0.6 s when a radar holds the target: it cues the seeker) and the main radar's `lock` for radar (0.7-2 s by part; `× (2 − eccm)` against a jammer). Firing a radar missile with no radar sends it unguided (one toast a flight); a lock on a new target past the radar's targets at once (`tws`) is refused ("Radar busy").

**`airframe(w)`** (combat.js:664) defaults, IR / radar:
- drag 1.6e-4 / 1.1e-4
- gimbal 0.6 / 1.1 rad
- track rate 0.7 / 1.0 rad/s
- bleed 0.012, arm 0.6 s, irccm 0, PN gain `nav` 4

**`guideAir`** (combat.js:765) uses proportional navigation. Maximum pull is `turn·g·min(1, q/QREF)` with `QREF = ½·1.225·400²` (combat.js:663). Energy losses are in `moveShot` (combat.js:533-536): steep drag above `vmax`, and bleed ∝ g² scaled by `QREF/q`, so turns cost more in thin air.

The target is dropped (`s.target = null`, missile flies dumb) when:
- the motor has burnt out and speed is below 150 m/s;
- the line of sight leaves the gimbal;
- the line-of-sight rate exceeds `track` for more than 0.25 s while farther than 60 m;
- for SARH: the launcher's radar loses the target for more than 1 s (a notch gets 0.4 s of grace). The launcher's radar is `Radar.sees(…, 'guide', s)` for a craft (its radar parts; radar.js), `siteSees` when the launcher is a range target, i.e. the frigate (`s.launcher.def`), or `Aim.radarSees` for a ground launcher's tracking radar (`s.gr`). An **active** missile (`s.act`, from `w.active`, with `s.link`: its radar has a data link, `guide` 2) inside `act` of its target goes on its own seeker (`s.pit`, never back) and from then asks only `clutter` from its own position, so the launcher may turn away (chapter 9 §6);
- a decoy wins.

**Beating missiles:**
- **Energy:** short burn, then drag and turn bleed.
- **IR signature** (`irSig` combat.js:673): `Σ firing·thrust·(1+1.5·ab)` (props and rotors ×0.5, and ×`def.irK` since PR #36, :678: the FS-22's flat nozzles 0.7 and the FS-35's serrated one 0.85 hide some heat) `/120 + 0.08`. It scales by aspect: tail 1, side 0.35, head-on 0.1, or 0.3 with the afterburner lit.
- **Flares** (`decoyed` combat.js:742): each flare is judged once per missile (`s.cm` WeakSet), within 0.3 rad of the line of sight. `K = 0.3·(1 − t/3)`, and the chance is `min(.85, K/(K+irSig))·(1−irccm)`. Throttling back helps.
- **Chaff** (radar missiles). Two kinds of cloud sit in `S.flares` with `chaff: true`, both living 5 s:
  - the **free cloud** that comes with flares (`flares(v, sim, true)`, combat.js:305: by hand when there is no dispenser bundle, or from `autoCM` against a radar missile), and
  - a **dispenser bundle** (`str: 2`, PR #35) from a Chaff Dispenser (`chaff(v, sim, p)`, combat.js:323: from the first dispenser with bundles left (`dispenser`, :322), at 0.5× the craft's velocity plus 6 m/s "down" for a plane, and `release()` takes a bundle and its 1 kg).
  - **Reach and odds** (`chaffR`, `chaffOdds`, combat.js:437-438; `vr` the target's speed along the beam, under 80 m/s = crossing it): the free cloud 250 m, 0.6 crossing, else 0.15; a bundle 400 m, 0.75 crossing, else 0.15. A bundle's edge over the free cloud is its reach, its waiting for the missile and the odds when crossing. (Its odds when not crossing were 0.25 until the review found that mashing `K` made radar missiles almost useless.)
  - **`decoyed`** (combat.js:742) weighs each cloud once per missile (`s.cm`), only within 0.15 rad of the line of sight and within `d + 800` m of the missile. The free cloud keeps its old order: marked as seen first, then it must lie within 250 m of the target, so one dropped early is wasted. A bundle is only marked as seen once every test passes, including the missile being within 4 km of the target (`d ≤ 4000`) and the cloud within 400 m of it: one dropped early waits, while it lasts, until the missile is close.
  - **`chaffBreaks(v, t)`** (combat.js:441): a cloud near a target the player's radar has locked can break the lock (each cloud once), with the same radius and odds. It covers bandits that drop chaff and other players' clouds.
  - Hit rates measured with the real missile code (200 trials a row, with the odds above): a radar missile fired from 8 km at a stock Falcon hits 84 % tail-on, 33.5 % across its path and 83 % head-on when the Falcon drops one bundle at 3 km, and 13 % / 0 % / 30.5 % when it mashes `K` inside 7 km. Against a bandit dropping its free cloud as the AI does: 50 % tail-on, 5.5 % across, 89 % head-on (100 % each before PR #35, when the AI dropped only flares).
- **Notching.** `radarSees` went in the big update; an aircraft's radar is `Radar.sees` (chapter 9 §4), built on the same pieces: `radarReach(R, t, d, eccm, st)` (combat.js:905: `R·(1−stealth)`, then against a jammer beyond 5 km `× (0.7 + 0.3·eccm)`, at least 5 km), the radar part's own cone about its own axis (it was 60° of the nose for every radar), the ground in the way (`clearLine` at a 400 m step), then `clutter(o, t, tp, dx, dy, dz, d, R)` (:912): a low target (50 m below the radar, or under 300 m above the ground) is `'clutter'` to a radar that can't look down (`R.down` false: the pulse sets), and `'notch'` to one that can if it moves along the beam slower than `R.notch` (40 m/s with no `R`: the frigate's rule, as before).
- **A ship's radar** (`siteSees(e, t, tp, memo)`, combat.js:718; PR #35) guides the frigate's SAMs: all round, from the mast (`mastOf`, :727: `def.h + 12` m up), out to `radarReach(e.def.radar)`, never through the ground: `clearLine(o, p, d)` (:730) samples the ground or sea every ~150 m (4-120 samples) and needs 4 m of clearance. The answer is kept on the missile for 0.25 s of its own clock (`memo.losAt`/`los`). Then `clutter`, so notching works against it. True, `'notch'` or false. At 1/20 scale the horizon hides a low craft too.
- **ECM** (`jams` combat.js:192):
  - radar reach ×0.7 beyond 5 km (minimum 5 km);
  - radar lock time ×2;
  - AA spread 0.012→0.03;
  - local enemy missiles more than 1.2 km away lose their target at 0.35/s (combat.js:497). That only affects SAM and AI missiles, never multiplayer ones.
  - Until PR #37 it also warned of heat seekers within 2 km in multiplayer. That is gone: only a warning receiver warns (below).
- **Stealth** (`stealthOf` combat.js:181): area-weighted `def.stealth` over the hull, minus 0.12 per external store, pod or ECM pod still carrying ammo, clamped to 0..0.75. Paint never counts. A Chaff Dispenser and a warning receiver (`d.rwr`, PR #37) count as neither hull nor store (they sit flush in the skin), so a Spectre with both is exactly as stealthy as without; nor does a radar set inside a nose (`innerRadar`, the big update), while a Radar Pod counts as a store. A `doors` part with its doors open (`p.deployed > 0.05`, :187; PR #36) counts as one store hung outside, so an internal bay keeps a jet stealthy until it shoots (as the templates come, one bay open): the FS-22 0.68 shut, 0.56 for a moment after a launch; the FS-35 0.64 and 0.52; the Sugoi-57 0.54 and 0.42 (its bays are hull with `stealth` 0.6). Internal bays without doors (`bombBay`) count as hull, as before.

**Countermeasures** (PR #35):
- **By hand:** `K` or the CM button calls `countermeasures(v, sim)` (combat.js:335), at most once per 0.6 s: the flares if fitted, and a bundle if the craft has a dispenser with bundles left. Flares bring their free cloud only when there is no bundle (`flares(v, sim, !dispenser)`), so a craft with flares and no dispenser (every design from before #35) behaves exactly as before, and once the dispenser is empty the free cloud comes back. True if either went.
- **By themselves:** `autoCM(v, sim, m)` (combat.js:345) drops, against a radar missile (`m.sarh || m.radar`), a dispenser bundle, or without one flares with their free cloud; against a heat seeker, flares alone, as before. AI fighters use it (`flyFighter`, under 1.8 km, every 0.7 s, combat.js:1069; the AI needs no warning receiver). A solo player's go out by themselves only with a warning receiver on board (below).

**Warnings: the warning receiver** (PR #37; combat.js:1153-1168). Until PR #37 every enemy missile homing on the player warned solo and set off the countermeasures; in multiplayer only radar missiles warned, and heat seekers within 2 km of an ECM pod. Now a missile is heard only by an RWR part, solo and in multiplayer, heat seekers too:
- `frame` sets `S.rwr = rwrOf(v)` (0 once `F.destroyed`). Every missile in `S.shots` with `s.target === v && s.enemy` is a threat; with `S.rwr` 0 it is skipped: no warning, no tone, nothing goes out by itself. With an RWR it sets `S.warn` (and `S.warnR` if radar-guided, `s.radar || s.sarh`) and joins `S.threats`, sorted nearest first (:1166).
- **Tones:** `Sound.beep(1500)` every 0.22 s while `S.warn`; otherwise, with an RWR and another player's radar holding us (`lockers()`, §1.7), a slow `Sound.tone(700, 0.2, 0.04)` once a second (`S.lockToneT`).
- **Solo** (`!mpOn()`) with an RWR, a threat within 2.2 km sets off the countermeasures every 0.6 s: with the Advanced RWR through `autoCM` (the right decoy for its kind), with the basic one through `countermeasures` (flares and a bundle, as `K` would, because it can't tell the kinds apart). In multiplayer nothing goes out by itself: press `K`.
- **Multiplayer:** a replica's `s.target` is our craft when the shooter's message names us (`tg === Net.me`) and the shooter is hostile. The launch (`msl`) set it before PR #37; now every update (`mp`, 10 a second) sets it again (`netMissileUpdate(pid, id, m, target)`, combat.js:838, from mp.js:197), so a missile that turns onto us after launch is heard from its next update, one that turns away goes quiet, and one fired at a craft we have since replaced follows the new one. The `mp` message always carried `tg`; only the receiver changed.
- **Without an RWR** the only cue is the red MSL marker over each hostile missile in sight within 4 km (§1.7). The descriptions of the radar missiles, flares and Chaff Dispenser say so.
- A radar lock on another player calls `MP.sendLock` (combat.js:1462), re-sent every 1 s, an LPI radar's (the AESA) only within its quiet reach (`Radar.heard`: 10.4 km). On their side it lands in `MP.rwr` (§3) and shows only with an RWR on board (§1.7).
- **Solo radar locks** (the big update): `lockers()` also returns `Radar.S.locks`: bandits within `6 km × (1 − stealth)` with us in their nose, the frigate while its SAM homes on us, the AI's SPAA whose tracking radar holds us. So RADAR LOCK, its tone and the rim strobes work flying solo too. An Advanced RWR also hears the radars painting us (`Radar.S.scanners`: faint search strobes) and reads an active radar missile as MISSILE ACTIVE (`s.pit`; `A` on its scope; "ACTIVE: chaff now · turn across its path").

**Ground seekers:** `guide()` (combat.js:801) is lead pursuit with `tgo ≤ 12 s`, turn rate `turn·g/v`, and `vmax` as a hard cap.

#### 1.5 Damage

- **`maxHp(def)`** (combat.js:44) = `def.hp` when the part has one (the ground update's parts), else `round((60 + 220·√(mass + fuel·0.25)) · (control ? 1.4 : 1))`. `p.hp` is lazily created.
- **`engineK`**: thrust is ×1 at ≥60% health, falling to 0.3 at 0. **`liftK`**: lift is ×1 at ≥70%, falling to 0.45. `hpFrac` measures a wing with half shot off (`p.cut`) against half its health: the half that's left is its own pool.
- **`damage(v, p, amt, cause, sim, by, side)`** (combat.js:52; `side`, since the big update, the half of an aircraft's plain wing the hit was on, 1 for +z and −1 for −z, when the shooter knows it: `explode` and `moveShot` work it out with `wingSide`, from where the blast or the round met the wing; a hit message from another player carries none yet, so the dice pick):
  - **does nothing for `v.remote`**: the owner applies damage to its own craft; nor (the big update) for `v.foreign` (someone else's falling hulk or its pieces on our screen: only a picture), nor to a dead engine or a knocked-out cockpit of an aircraft (`Death.partDead`: nothing more to take);
  - (the big update) on an aircraft's first hit records what it had (`Death.base`: its lift, tail surfaces, fins, live engines, rotors, and whether anyone flew it), against which crippled and down are measured; a hit by the player (`Combat.byMe`) on another aircraft or ground vehicle ticks the air HUD's hit marker (`Death.hit`); an aircraft's engine or fuel part knocked under 30% by a hit (the air rule's gate: never a crash's dent or a crash's own blast) rolls for fire (`Death.ignite`, §1.5), and the player's toast says what happened: "<part> on fire! Throttle back or dive to put it out" ("Dive" for a fuel fire) or "<part> badly damaged" (every other kind keeps "on fire!"); a fire's own damage (cause `fire`) ticks no hit marker and says nothing more as it burns the part down;
  - (PR #38) does nothing to a dead crewman's seat, or within 4 s of a battle spawn (`v.spawnAt`); on a gv vessel sets `v.hurtAt` (repairs wait 6 s after it); a gv part at 0 hp goes to `Armour.moduleDown` (a module breaks and stays; a hull or turret knocks the vehicle out, the hulk staying) instead of `destroyPart`;
  - armour scales damage by `(1 − armor)`;
  - sets `v.lastHitBy` and `v.lastHitAt`;
  - toasts at 60% and 30% for the player;
  - calls `destroyPart` at 0 hp.
- **`holdRips`/`flushRips`** (combat.js:116, PR #38): while a hit, a blast or a hit message lands, the ground update's parts it breaks off (bricks, skirts, slats, plates: nothing that explodes) wait and go in one `ripParts` call per craft at the end (and, since the big update, an aircraft is then asked whether it is crippled or down, `Death.afterAir`). `RIP.id` numbers each window (`Combat.ripWin()`: the window's number while one is open, else 0), so the air rule's rare detonation rolls once per craft per hit event. Old parts break off as before.
- **`destroyPart(v, p, cause, sim, side)`** (combat.js:80). Its lines run in a fixed order, a slot for each package of the big update: a module part goes to `Armour.moduleDown`; then **an aircraft's part takes the air rule** (below; when it returns true nothing else runs, and `'det'` runs the old body below inside the detonation's window and then `Death.detonated`); then `Death.groundPart` (an old car's or the Technical's cab: its driver knocked out, ground section below); then the old body:
  - Parts with thrust, more than 50 kg of fuel, rocket/missile/bomb ammo, or `control` go through `sim.breakParts` (an explosion). Everything else goes through `sim.ripParts` (torn off as debris; flight.js:258, 372).
  - Fuel over 500 kg adds a blast of radius `4+√(fuel/n)·0.08`, dmg 45.
  - Remaining stores blast at radius `blast·0.6`, dmg `dmg·0.5/count`.
  - **A legacy twin pair** (spread equal to `def.twinZ` within 5e-4) **blasts once**, at the centre (z = 0).
  - **A split twin** (any other spread) **blasts at each copy**, with fuel and store damage divided by the number of blast points. If only one store of a pair is left, it blasts only on the side due to fire next.
  - These secondary blasts carry `by = null`, so they never credit anyone and never reach remote craft.
  - (the big update) A crash's dent or a burn-up (cause `impact` or `heat`) runs the old body inside `Death.DET.crash`, so the fuel and stores it sets off break every aircraft they reach the old way too: a crash is no hit. After a hit's old body on an aircraft (Hollywood, or a part the air rule passed on), `Death.afterAir` asks whether it is crippled or down.
- **`explode(b, at, blast, dmg, by, sim, quiet, he, spare)`** (combat.js:196; `he`, the blast's penetration against armour, and `spare`, a craft it leaves alone such as a launcher at its own muzzle, since PR #38):
  - A plated craft's parts are measured where their rings turn them and go through `Armour.blastK` (chapter 8 §6); its module rows go out with the hit message. Wrecks take nothing.
  - Falloff `dmg·(1 − d/blast)^1.5`, where d is the distance to the nearest copy of the part minus `max(w,h)/2`.
  - If `by` is the local player's craft: blasts of 8 m or more go out as `MP.sendBoom`, and remote craft in range collect `[part, dmg]` lists, sent as one `MP.sendHits(..., 'blast')` per craft.
  - Range targets are skipped in multiplayer.
  - (the big update) Someone else's falling hulk (`v.foreign`) takes nothing; an aircraft's plain wing's entry carries the side the blast is on (`wingSide`).
- **Crash damage** happens in `Vessel.ground` (vessel.js:931-968):
  - the limit is `breakSpeed` (default 9, vessel.js:9), ×1.8 on water;
  - with combat loaded, a part breaks above 1.6× the limit and dents above 0.75× (`Combat.impact` combat.js:71: `maxHp·(ratio−0.75)·1.6`, at most once per 0.4 s per part);
  - a spinning prop or rotor touching the ground shatters;
  - the silly flag `bouncy` disables dents.
- **Smoke and leaks** (`frame`'s 0.07 s tick), for craft within 5 km, ghosts included: smoke below 60% health, fire below 30%, fuel leaks below 50%; since the big update a part `Death.partFX` smokes its own way is left out (an aircraft's: a grey trail, no fire unless it burns; Hollywood keeps the old smoke), and `Death.tick(F, e, n)` runs first on each tick (`e` the real time since the last: the fires' timers and damage, the trails). `integrity(v)` feeds the HUD.

**What 0 hp does: the air rule** (the big update's damage model, js/death.js). The hp maths stay combat.js's (`maxHp`, the blast's fall-off, `liftK`, `engineK`); death.js decides what a part at zero does. Before it, an aircraft's engine, fuel tank or cockpit at 0 blew the whole craft up (`breakParts`): one missile, one fireball. Now an aircraft is shot apart as in War Thunder:
- **The gate.** `Death.airRule(v, p, cause)`: an aircraft (`isAirKind`: `AIR_KINDS` in parts.js, `plane`, `heli` and the coming `drone`), an old part (not `def.gv`), the cause a hit (`shot`, `blast`, `fire`; not `impact` or `heat`, not inside a crash's old body, `DET.crash`), and Hollywood off. Rockets, cars, tanks, a winged rocket, Hollywood and every crash keep the old body exactly. `airHit(v, cause)` is the same test for the craft.
- **`Death.airPart(v, p, cause, sim, side)`**, in order:
  1. **The rare mid-air detonation**: a part holding over 500 kg of fuel or loaded stores, overkilled by at least half its health (`DEATH.over`), on the event's one roll (`detRoll`: once per craft per hit window, never from the detonation's own blasts, `DET.n`): 10%, 20% for a loaded bomb. It returns `'det'`: the old body runs (the craft breaks up, the fuel and store blasts), then `detonated` throws the pieces out tumbling, lights those holding fuel (`ignite`), shows one `air` fireball (`deathFX`) and, ours in a room, sends it as a boom.
  2. **A big tank burnt through** (cause `fire`, over 500 kg, 10% on its own dice, salt 950): the same, with "Fuel tank exploded!".
  3. **The cockpit**: the pilot is knocked out (`p.ko`, the part kept at 0 hp, a canopy puff). With no live control part the craft falls limp: `Vessel.keepPart` keeps the knocked-out cockpit's piece as the craft when it splits (`splitComponents`, `Flight.ripParts`).
  4. **A main rotor** (a helicopter's): torn off, it falls.
  5. **An engine**: dead and kept aboard (`active` false, at 0 hp; a pair is one part), "<engine> out!", and a roll for fire.
  6. **A main wing**: half of it goes (`cutWing`): `p.cut` is 1 (its +z half gone) or −1, from `side` or else the part's dice. The overkill carries over to the half left (`hp = maxHp/2 + overkill`): past it the whole wing goes. What hangs on that half alone (one copy out on that side and what hangs on it) falls with a new part of the same type, cut the other way, holding half the fuel; twin pairs and old designs' parts with no `att` stay with the plane. Vessel.js gives a cut wing half its area in `clS` and lifts only the half that is left (`surf` skips the gone half; the main wing's angle is read off the other), so the plane spirals; hit boxes (`boxesOf`), the 3D rig (`cutRig`, chapter 3 §3) and the wingtip condensation streaks (flight.js) follow. A second zero on a cut wing tears it off.
  7. **Anything else**: torn off (`ripParts`), with a roll for fire if it holds over 50 kg of fuel.
- **Crippled or down** (`afterAir` → `airCheck`, after every outcome and after a hit's old body): `state(v)` against `v.air0`. **Down** (destroyed now, limp: no stick, no autopilot, throttle 0, engines off; the player's flight ends through `onDestroyed`): the pilot out, under 30% of the lift left (`DEATH.downLift`), or every main rotor gone. **Crippled** (the stick stays): under 75% of the lift (a half wing), every tail surface gone (not on a delta with elevons), every fin, the tail rotor (`v.tailLost`: the main rotor's reaction spins the helicopter, `DEATH_TAIL_YAW` 0.12 of its thrust × its radius, fading out at 3.2 rad/s), or every engine. It sets `v.hulk` and the credit `v.doomBy` (the last hitter within 20 s, once), toasts the player once per state ("Tail shot off!", "All engines out"…), puts an AI bandit down at once (`Combat.aiDown`), and when the player did it (`Combat.byMe`) sets the air HUD's banner (`PILOT KO`, `SHOT DOWN`, `WING OFF`…; a bandit's kill outranks its `PILOT KO`: `aiDown` raises `SHOT DOWN` first). The pieces a hit makes are tagged (`tagPieces`): an aircraft's as hulks, anything else's but a rocket's as junk.
- **Dice, not random streams**: every roll that changes the simulation is `Death.dice(p, salt)`, a hash of the part's id (the same part and salt, the same outcome; no random stream consumed, so seeded tests and other craft never shift); pictures use `FX.rnd`.
- **`Combat.byMe(o, sim)`** is the one "the player did it" test (the player's craft, or one it carries: `o.carrier`, for drones) and **`Combat.isFighter(o)`** the one "an AI bandit" test (an AI aircraft that is neither the ground AI's nor a drone).
- **Saves**: a cut wing saves `cut` (only 1 or −1, only on a plain wing: `airLoad` drops anything else), a spinning helicopter `tailLost` (only with rotors and no tail rotor); fires are never saved, and a knocked-out pilot comes back from the cockpit's own health at 0 or under.
- **Fire** (death.js's fire section, `FIRE`). `Death.ignite(v, p, odds, by, sim, quiet)` lights one part on its dice (`dice(p, p.fireN)` under the odds, `p.fireN` counting the rolls; it burns `8 + 14·dice(p, 100 + fireN)` s, `p.burn`, lit by `p.burnBy`): a hit taking an engine under 30% 40%, a fuel part over 50 kg 30% (combat.js's line, `quiet`: it toasts itself); an engine at 0 60% (rule 5); a fuel part torn off 50% (rule 7, `quiet: 'piece'`: no toast, no banner), as do a falling half wing and a detonation's pieces holding fuel. Never on someone else's craft (`remote`, `foreign`), a ground-update part, a drone (grams of battery), anything the air rule's gate shuts out (rockets, cars, tanks, Hollywood, a crash: `airHit`), nor a part already burning. Lit on the player's craft: a toast (unless `quiet`) and a buzz; on a craft we lit that someone flies: the `ON FIRE` banner. `fireTick` (the first part of `Death.tick`, every vessel on the active craft's body, no distance cap): a peer's craft's fire is only a picture (its owner runs it); in water it goes out at once; on a piece or a limp hulk (`foreign`, no control, debris) its timer runs and it hurts nothing; otherwise the timer runs ×3 for an engine fire at throttle 0.2 or less and ×3 again above 150 m/s EAS (`sqrt(dynP/0.6)`), and a live part loses 4% of its health a second, applied after the loop as `Combat.damage(…, 'fire', F, p.burnBy)` (so a part lit at 30% is gone in 7.5 s, and the credit is the lighter's); "<part>: fire out" when the player's goes out. No spread, never saved (`airLoad` drops `burn`, `burnBy`, `fireN`). The pictures: behind each burning part black smoke and a flame (`FX.trail(…, 'black', n, e)`, `FX.flame`), behind each leaking one (fuel left, under 50% health, not burning: `Death.leaking`) white; the 4 nearest within 3 km of the camera at full rate (`n = ceil(speed·e/space)`, a puff every 7 m on High and 14 on Low, at most 4 or 2 a tick), the rest to 8 km one puff and one flame every 2nd tick, none further or above 4× warp; a pair's copies and a wing's halves (a cut wing's remaining half) in turn. Measured at 250 m/s: about 64 live puffs a trail on Low (death.test D1). `airPartFX` (the first half of `Death.partFX`): an aircraft's other damaged parts (60% or under) lay a grey puff every 2nd tick within 3 km and no fire particle. `Sound.crackle(at)` (audio.js): a 40 ms band-passed click every 0.15-0.35 s while a part of the player's craft burns. `Death.burning(v)` (an engine first) and `Death.leaking(p)` are for the HUD.
- **Falls and crashes** (death.js's crash and lifecycle section). A **hulk** is an aircraft crippled or down, a piece shot off one, or a peer's falling hulk on our screen (`v.hulk`: the sim time it became one). It falls under the ordinary physics; Flight steps it to `DEATH.hulkFar` (12 km) from the active craft instead of 6 km (`Flight.stepsNear`), and a limp one ignores the stick (`Flight.inert`: no controls, no staging, no part used, no flaps or gear: `cycleFlaps`/`toggleGear` return at once and their pills hide). `impactAir` (the crash hook, `Flight.breakParts`' first line for cause `impact`): a hulk hitting anything (or a crippled one hitting faster than 40 m/s or breaking its cockpit), and any aircraft whose cockpit breaks outside Hollywood, goes up in **one fireball** where it is, brought down onto what is under its centre (the ground, the sea or a deck: `Terrain.surfaceAt`) when that is within its reach + 2 m, so a banked jumbo's tip strike burns on the ground, not 14 m up (`crash`: `FX.death` `'crash'`, `'thud'` for a cold piece with no fuel, engine or fire, a splash and steam in water), and a big hot one (size ≥ 2.5 m) leaves a **crash site** (`pyre`: `Death.S.pyres`, `{b, x, y, z, t, k, wet}`, the newest 6): the 3 nearest within 6 km burn 30 s (a flame every 2nd tick, a column puff every 6th, 12th on Low), then smoke to 75 s; a wet one steams 10 s. The crash hurts nothing (the old crash never did, and a peer's hulk landing near us must not), marks the vessel `dead` (the flight's cleanup drops it), puts an AI bandit down and, for the player's craft, sets `Flight.crashedAt` and ends the flight. Gear or a wing tip broken on landing keeps today's per-part breakage. `hulkTick` (in `Death.tick`): a limp hulk still for 2 s on the ground or the water (under 3 m/s) goes up if anything in it can burn, else becomes cold junk; any hulk still about after 150 s goes up where it is (in the air, if it is still up there); a crippled craft whose pilot lands it and stands still 3 s lives (its `hulk` and credit `doomBy` cleared). **Junk** (`v.junkAt`: cold pieces at rest, torn-off ground kit, `Death.groundPieces`) lies 90 s once still, then sinks over 6 s (`v.sink` 0..1, the renderer lowers it) and goes; past 40 at rest the oldest sink at once. All of it runs on the sim's clock, for local vessels only (a peer's craft is its owner's). `Death.reset` (from `Combat.reset`) clears the banner and the crash sites, but a respawn (a room's, or one keeping vessels: the Practice Battle's) keeps them.
- **Ground vehicles** (the ground section). Every way in is one knock-out with the hulk staying. `groundPart`: an old control part (an old car's cab, the Technical's) at 0 hp from a hit, a blast or a fire keeps its cab aboard at 0 (`p.ko`) and knocks the driver out: `Armour.knockOut(v, sim, 'crew')` for a vehicle with the ground update's parts (the Technical), `Death.groundKO` for an old car, which never calls into the ground update's files (the zero-call contract): `v.ko = {why, x, t}`, every control part `ko`, throttle 0, the HUD's KNOCKED OUT when we did it, "Driver knocked out!" (or "Wrecked!") for the player, the small `'ko'` death effect, the flight's end, an AI's `aiDown`. `impactGround` (the crash hook): a hull, turret, crew seat or cab broken in a crash knocks the vehicle out (`'hull'`), keeps those parts aboard at 0 and freezes it at once (a crash returns before the ground pushes back: stepped on, it would sink); one standing on its nose or tail (its forward axis more than 30° off level) is laid down first (`layDown`: pitched about its own lateral axis onto its tracks, onto its roof only if it leaned that way by more than 15°, its lowest point put on the ground), and render3d sinks a wreck by its standing height as it lies; a thrown turret landing hard just lies there. `groundTick` (in `Death.tick`): a knocked-out hulk of ours settles into a wreck (`settle`: `v.wreck`, frozen, solid, charred by the renderer) once it is on the ground or in water and under 2 m/s, or 6 s after the knock-out; a thrown turret once it lies still; then **`wreckFX`** for the 3 knocked-out vehicles nearest the camera within 4 km (ours, the AI's, peers' wrecks; none above 4× warp): from its burn point (the engine's top or the hull's it sits in; an old car's knocked-out cab) a flame each tick and a column puff every 6th (12th on Low) for 20 s (40 after an ammunition detonation), then the column every 20th tick to two minutes, then a thin grey wisp every 40th until it sinks: about 30 live particles a burning wreck, 6 a smoking one. `koPartFX` (`partFX`'s ground half): a knocked-out vehicle's parts give none of the generic damage smoke (its hull at 0 no longer smokes forever). `Armour.knockOut` calls `Death.deathFX` once (`'ammo'` on a detonation, else `'ko'`) and draws exactly as many `vrnd()` as before, so armour.test's seeded tosses are unchanged. Lifetimes (every mode): GroundAI's list and MP's (`MP_WRECKS` 8, 300 s, the last 6 sinking with its thrown turret; a ninth standing starts the oldest sinking now; chapter 8 §9 and §12). Hollywood keeps the old breakage (cars blow up, ×3).
- **The HUD and the end of a life** (the HUD section; css/style.css's damage section). `Death.hit(sim)` ticks four short diagonals 16-22 px round the gun cross for 0.15 s after each of our hits on an aircraft or ground vehicle (`Combat.byMe`; a fire's damage ticks nothing) and raises `HIT` when no banner is up; `Death.feedback(text, sim)` raises one banner at a time from `FB` (`HIT` 0.6 s; `ENGINE OUT`, `ON FIRE`, `WING OFF`, `TAIL OFF`, `FIN OFF`, `ROTOR OFF`, `ENGINE OFF` 1.5 s; `PILOT KO` 2.2 s; `SHOT DOWN` and `KNOCKED OUT` 2.2 s, bigger, with a chime unless in a ground vehicle's sight or a room, where the kill's message chimes): a lower one never replaces a higher one still showing. `drawHits` (from `Combat.drawHUD`) draws them round `Combat.aimCross` (the middle of the screen when it is behind the camera), the banner 48 px over the cross (a kill's 74), below it when that would run into the panels at the top (`#flight-ui .telemetry`, `.flight-top-right`, `#mp-bar`, `#gai-bar`, measured once a banner), beside them when both would (the cross beside or under one, in Aim or looking round: the nearest place clear of them, above or below), 8 px inside the sides, fading in its last third of a second; nothing to show, no work; never in a ground vehicle's sight (GHUD shows its own) nor once destroyed. **Merged toasts**: the player's own losses inside one hit window (`Combat.ripWin`) or one hit (`holdLost`/`flushLost` round `damage`) wait in `Death.S.lost` (`lose`) and go as one toast with one bang and one buzz ("Right wing, Pylon and 2 more shot off! Jet Engine out!", `lostText`). **The end of a life** (flight.js): `onDestroyed` sets `diedAt` and `deathText` (`deathLine`: Ammo detonation, Wrecked, Knocked out, Pilot knocked out, Shot down, Crashed, Destroyed, "Drone lost", and "by <name>": the credited killer `doomBy`, else the last hitter within 20 s, 24 letters) and shows **the strip** (`#flight-alert.lost`: "Pilot knocked out · by Bandit · tap for options", Esc on a desktop; placed by `placeAlert` clear of every control and panel). Every end screen (this flight's 1.6 s timer, a room's 1400-1800 ms ones, the Practice Battle's panel) runs through **`Flight.afterFall(cb)`**, polled every 250 ms: once the craft has stopped falling and 1.2 s after its crash (`DEATH.endAfter`), at most 10 s (`endMax`) after the death; a tap or click on the strip (`endNow`) brings it at once. The follow camera eases back to 1.4× its distance over the first second of the fall (render3d `deathEase`). **While alive and hurt** (`updateHUD`): the damage row reads Knocked out, Pilot KO, Shot down, `Crippled · n%` or `On fire · n%`; the alert line ranks a breaking airframe (over-g or overspeed at level 2) > a fire ("Engine fire · throttle back or dive", "Fire · dive to put it out") > the level-1 warnings > "Fuel leak".
- **Credit.** `v.doomBy` is set once, when an aircraft first becomes a hulk, from `lastHitBy` within 20 s; it outlives a long fall or a fire (a fire's damage is credited to `p.burnBy`, the hitter that lit it), and goes if the crippled craft lands safely. MP's `down.by` and the strip read it first.
- **What moved.** No baseline moved in Phase 2 (every key of the eight baseline files is identical to 68ea3fe's). Re-pinned on purpose: armour.test.js:444 (the hulk's own smoke is `wreckFX`'s, `partFX` gives none), groundai.test.js:67-68 (a practice wreck stays its five minutes, sinking from 294 s), mp.test.js:350-353 (8 standing plus sinking, 300 s) and :394 (`NET_VERSION` 11), ground-models.mjs (an old part charred too). The AI fighters' pinned chases (combat.test.js), every old design's flight without hits, `mp-air.json` and the zero-call contract (the buggy's knock-out included) are unchanged.

#### 1.6 Target range, SAMs and AI fighters (single-player only)

**`TARGET_KINDS`** (combat.js:8):

| Kind | hp | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| tank | 600 | |
| truck | 220 | |
| radar | 320 | emitter |
| bunker | 1600 | |
| aa | 380 | aa, emitter |
| sam | 420 | sam, emitter |
| boat | 520 | |
| ship (Frigate) | 3200 | aa, emitter, `rsam`, `radar: 13000` (its own radar guides its SAMs, combat.js:16) |
| drone | 140 | air, 75 m/s orbits |

- **Layout and spawning:** counts come from `TARGET_LAYOUT` (combat.js:19). Targets spawn when the player is on Earth within 60 km of `at(8000,0,0)` and the terrain map is ready (combat.js:1110-1114).
- **Placement** (`place` combat.js:910) uses a seeded RNG:
  - land: 45% on Shetland, else the Faroes; height 2–220 m, off the runway and space centre, gentle slope;
  - sea: depth below −12 m;
  - drones orbit a 900–1800 m radius at 500–1400 m.
- Dead targets respawn elsewhere after 60 s.
- **Static targets** (no `def.speed`: radars, bunkers, AA guns, SAM sites) keep their `pos` and `h` while `e`/`z` still match `posE`/`posZ`, so they don't look up the ground every frame (`syncTarget` combat.js:936, 956).

**Hostile mode:**
- **AA** (`aaFire` combat.js:962): fires 3 s out of every 7 when the player is within `3500·(1−0.6·stealth)`. Tracers at 850 m/s, dmg 6, leading the player.
- **SAM** (`samFire` combat.js:973): fires when the player is within `9000·(1−stealth)` and above 150 m. First launch after 4 s, then every 22 s, one missile in the air per site. Since PR #37 no toast announces it (it said "SAM launch!" within 12 km): only a warning receiver tells. The missile is an IR seeker: burn 5, accel 220, vmax 950, 22 g, dmg 300, blast 13, arm 0.4 s. SAM sites stay heat seekers (the README promises flares beat them).
- **Frigate radar SAM** (PR #35; `shipSamFire` combat.js:985, called from `frame` at 1111-1116):
  - Wakes when the player is within `def.radar` (13 km) of the frigate and above 150 m (as for the SAM sites, so a take-off from the runway, which the ship lies off, isn't met by one).
  - First look after 20 s (`t.rsamT`), then it fires only if `siteSees(...) === true` (in reach for the craft's stealth and ECM, in clear view, not notching), else looks again every 1 s. After a launch it waits 13 s, and the ship has at most one missile in the air (one that went dumb counts until it falls).
  - The missile: launched from the mast along the line of sight plus 0.6 × up (it climbs steeply off the deck), `t 0.35`, life 30, burn 6, accel 210, vmax 1100, turn 22, dmg 300, blast 14, `radar: true` with `launcher: ship` (so `guideAir` rides `siteSees` all the way), airframe radar with drag 1e-4 and arm 0.4. `big: true` gives it a thick white trail (5 s puffs, `moveShot` combat.js:539) and a 1.5× model (render3d `syncCombat`).
  - Chaff (both kinds), notching, an island in the way and the ECM pod (which also makes SAMs lose their target now and then) beat it. Its launch toast ("Radar SAM launch! Drop chaff, or fly low across it") went in PR #37: an Advanced RWR reads RADAR MISSILE and says what to do, the basic one flashes MISSILE, and without one only its MSL marker shows.
- **Fighters:**
  - `RAIDER` (combat.js:21) is a Falcon-like jet layout with a cannon and flares.
  - `spawnFighter` (combat.js:996) places one 12–16 km away and 1.5–2.5 km up, flying at 230 m/s. Since the big update the range controller calls it as it spawns (`Radar.call`: "Bandit inbound: 14 km, your 10 o'clock"), since without a radar part the player sees it only within eye range.
  - At most 2 alive. Spawning repeats every 45 s (the first after 20 s, from `reset`) and needs the player airborne. Since PR #38 a tank counts as airborne only after more than 1 s off the ground and 15 m up (so driving over a crest brings no fighters, AA fire or SAMs; cars as before), and fighters ignore the ground AI.
  - **`flyFighter`** (combat.js:1026):
    - engages within `16 km·(1−stealth)` using lead pursuit (its implicit radar, which the big update kept: RAIDER has no radar part and the chases are pinned);
    - otherwise orbits `at(9000,0,2500)`;
    - ground avoidance takes priority;
    - bank-to-turn: roll the lift onto the target, then pull;
    - fires its gun when `d < 1100` and the aim is off by less than 0.05 rad;
    - drops countermeasures against a missile within 1.8 km, every 0.7 s, through `autoCM`: chaff (its Flares part's free cloud) against a radar missile, flares against a heat seeker. So the player's radar missiles now miss a bandit sometimes (§1.4).
  - Losing its control part, or the air rule's crippled or down (`airCheck`), calls `aiDown`: since the big update a bandit shot down falls limp (no stick, no throttle, no autopilot: `isDebris`, a hulk), crashes in one fireball and goes, so six kills in four minutes leave no pile of bandits and pieces (`candidates` skip `ai.down`; the spawn counts only live ones). Fighters are removed when the mode is not hostile or the player leaves the range. A target drone shot down (`hitTarget` keeps its way, `t.fv`) falls under gravity and drag, burning and trailing black smoke (`fallTarget`), and ends in a small fireball where it meets the ground or the sea within 60 s; it used to hang in the sky, a black model, until the next one came.
- **Kills:** `S.kills` counts kills where `Combat.byMe(v.lastHitBy, sim)` (the player's craft, or one it carries), with the toast and the SHOT DOWN banner.

**Countermeasures you can see** (PR #35; `cmFX(F, dt)` combat.js:1078, run from `frame`):
- A flare trails fire at a fixed 54 sparks a second (the old 60 Hz look) whatever the frame rate: an accumulator `f.fxN`, at most 6 a frame, spread back along the frame's path so a slow frame doesn't clump them. Before, it was about one per rendered frame, so a 120 Hz screen made twice as many.
- A chaff cloud bursts once, the first frame it exists (`f.shown`), in `chaffBurst` (:1092): 12 glints and 3 big soft grey puffs growing from 2 m to 14 m for a bundle, 8 and 2 (to 10 m) for the free cloud. The glints are `FX` kind `glint`, flashing for 3-5 s as they turn in the light (`FX.twinkle`). This replaced the faint grey smoke the free cloud trailed, about 18 puffs a second.
- Nothing is made for flares or clouds more than 3 km from `F.camAbs`.
- One `K` press of a Falcon now makes 447 particles at any frame rate (it was 500 at 60 Hz and 1024 at 120 Hz), and a cloud's overdraw-heavy smoke went from 74-178 puffs to 3.

#### 1.7 The HUD and the warning receiver's display

The HUD (`drawHUD` combat.js:1398) draws target brackets, Bandit diamonds, the seeker circle and LOCK, a 600 m gun pipper and LEAD marker, the bombsight (`bombImpact` combat.js:1608), the missile markers and, last, the warning receiver's display (`drawRWR`). A plane in Aim draws no pipper here: its one cross (`Flight.drawFlyAim`, `aimCross`) is the pipper. Since PR #38, aiming a ground vehicle, the pipper and LEAD marker step aside for the ground HUD's own marks (ghud.js, chapter 8 §11), the islands' far targets aren't bracketed along a ground vehicle's horizon, and the ground AI gets no Bandit diamond. Before PR #37 it wrote MISSILE or RADAR MISSILE itself whenever `S.warn` was set. Since the big update (chapter 9 §6): bandits get a diamond within eye range (`Radar.eye`: 6 km less half their stealth) and a hollow square when only our radar holds them, the pick white with ▸; a target drone is bracketed only once seen (by eye 4 km, or radar), the other range targets out to `clamp(radarOf × 0.7, 14 km, 32 km)`; a mast's ground contacts are small amber squares; LOCK carries its distance; LEAD reaches 3 km on an aircraft the radar holds (2 km else, the pick first), and the radar holding the gun's target inside 900 m turns the pipper (or Aim's cross) yellow. Each air marker drawn is registered for taps (`Radar.mark`).
- **MSL markers** (:1282-1295): over every missile within `SEE_MSL` (4 km, :1300) on our body and in front of the camera for which `hostileMissile(s, v)` (:1308) holds, a small red triangle and `MSL` (alpha 1 under 2 km, else 0.8). `hostileMissile`: a missile, not our own; anything homing on us (a team-mate's too, with friendly fire); another player's when `MP.enemy(Net.me, s.net)`; else `s.enemy` (the AI's and the sites'). It needs no RWR: it is the only way to see a missile coming without one.
- **`hudText(ctx, t, x, y, rim)`** (:1302): text with a dark rim (`strokeText` under `fillText`), so it reads over cloud and sea. The markers and the display use it.
- **`drawRWR(ctx, W, H, v, F)`** (:1703), nothing without an RWR, a radar part or a tail set's CHECK SIX. With a radar part aboard (not aiming a ground vehicle) the scope is the radar's (`Radar.draw`, bigger: chapter 9 §7) and the RWR's marks go on it (`rwrMarks`); a tail set's amber CHECK SIX ‹distance› shows with or without an RWR. An old design without a radar draws exactly what it drew:
  - Basic (`S.rwr` 1): with a threat, a flashing (250 ms) red **MISSILE**, 22 px, at `B.ty`; with no threat but a radar lock on us, a smaller flashing **RADAR LOCK**. No kind, no direction.
  - Advanced (`S.rwr` 2): the nearest threat's kind, **HEAT SEEKER** (`s.heat`), **RADAR MISSILE** (`s.radar || s.sarh`) or MISSILE; under it its distance, the seconds to impact (distance over closing speed, shown above 30 m/s) and `+n more`, then in yellow what to do, from `rwrAdvice(v, s, d)` (:1323): a heat seeker beyond 2.5 km "throttle back · flares at 2 km", closer "FLARES NOW · break turn" (or "break turn hard" with no flares left); a radar missile beyond 4.5 km "put it on your wing and dive (notch)", closer "CHAFF NOW · keep it on your wing" with a dispenser, "FLARES (chaff cloud) · notch it" with only flares, else "notch it: on your wing, low"; anything else "turn hard and get low".
  - The Advanced RWR's **scope**: a disc of radius `B.R` at `(B.x, B.y)`, heading up in the local horizontal (the nose projected off the local up; straight up or down it takes the belly's way), our craft a white triangle in the middle, a ring at half radius and a cross. Each radar lock is a red strobe on the rim (0.9 R) towards the locker's ghost; each threat a red triangle pointing in, at `0.28 + 0.6·clamp(d/12 km)` of the radius, labelled IR, R or M (A for an active radar missile on its own seeker, the big update); each radar painting us (`Radar.S.scanners`) a faint tick on the rim (0.95 R). These marks are `rwrMarks(ctx, B, at, locks, v)` (:1760), shared with the radar's scope. The rim turns red while anything threatens. "RWR" under it.
- **`lockers()`** (:1682): the live ghosts of the players in `MP.rwr` (their lock reports, mp.js:200) and, since the big update, the solo locks radar.js finds (`Radar.S.locks`: bandits, the frigate, AI SPAA; §1.4). It takes no argument.
- **`rwrPlace(W, H, rad)`** (:1790) finds room for the display (`rad`: the radar's scope, bigger, also clear of `.flight-top-right`, `#warp` and `#hint`: chapter 9 §7), once a second or when the canvas size changes (it reads the panels' `getBoundingClientRect`), and leaves the answer in `S.rwrBox = {x, y, R, ty, W, H, rad}` (and `lines` for a radar's label) for the tests and for `Radar.tap`. `S.rwrBox` holds only while a scope is drawn: `drawHUD` (and `Combat.noHUD`, called by main.js in the 2D view) clears it first each frame, `drawRWR` puts it back, and `reset` clears it, so a tap never lands on a scope that isn't there (an old design after a radar jet, the 2D view):
  - The lines go at `H·0.3`, or 26 px below `#flight-ui .telemetry` or `#mp-bar` where a 300 × 66 px box there would run under one.
  - The scope (R 40 px under 500 px wide, else 46) takes the first of these spots that is on the screen and clear of the room's bar, the lines, the flight data and `.left-controls`: under the flight data and above the controls (below the room's radar scope, `MPUI.scope`, when that is drawn: in a room, 761 px wide and up; on a narrow screen also below the lines); beside the room's scope; beside the flight data; smaller under the flight data (`Rs ≥ 28`); beside the left controls. With none free it takes the first.
  - `test/browser/rwr-hud.mjs` checks the result on five screens (chapter 6).

---

### 2. net.js: transport

- **Constants:**
  - `NET_VERSION = 11` (net.js:11). Version 11 (the big update, its one bump): aircraft shot apart and falling (`down.fall`, a cut wing's 13th `pv` column, a burning part's 3rd `hp` column, a hit row's wing side), wrecks on every screen in every mode (`down.w`, and the host's `wrecks` for a player joining late), radar parts (sets inside noses, a pod, a mast, active radar missiles: parts an older peer would drop), a radar switched off (`rdr`) and an active radar missile on its own seeker (`mp.a`), drones (the craft index `c` on a second craft's messages: a player's drone from their tank's bay flying beside it; `tc` on a missile after one; extras that never book a death, a kill or a ticket; chapter 10 §11) and the drone and counter-drone parts (parts an older peer would drop); an older peer would see a fireball where we see a falling hulk, and a whole wing where half is gone (§3). Version 10 (PR #44): the part limit is a room setting (`settings.cap`; with it off, an older peer would drop the craft past 600 parts and see a different world). Version 9 (PR #39): every shot and craft meets the props, and the props knocked over are shared (`crush`, `fallen`: chapter 8 §14); 8: the props themselves. Version 7 (PR #38) is the ground update: the Armour kind and its parts and paints, armour thickness in spawn rows, shells, module hits, turret rings in part states, `gd` in snapshots, ground battles (`obj`, `seen`/`spot`, respawns, wrecks on `down`), ground-launched missiles (chapter 8 §9). Version 2 added part depth, paint and host (`att`) to spawn rows; version 3 sends big messages in pieces (below); version 4 (PR #35) has the Chaff Dispenser, a part an older peer would drop from spawn rows (so it would see, and shoot at, a different craft), and the chaff strength in `cm`; version 5 (PR #36) has the look-alike jets' 96 parts and 13 paints, which an older peer would drop (`hasOwn(PARTS, …)`) or show in a part's own colours (`cleanSkin`); version 6 (PR #37) has the two warning receivers and the manned turret, parts an older peer would drop, and turrets that hold fire on the ground (an older peer's parked turrets would still shoot, and their hits count, §3). The host rejects a `hello` with any other `ver` (net.js:171).
  - `NET_MAX_BYTES = 256 KB` (net.js:12): the host drops, without relaying, any client message whose `JSON.stringify` length is larger (net.js:188). The host's own sends and clients' sends are not size-checked.
  - Room codes are 5 characters from `ROOM_CHARS` (net.js:13; no I, O, 0 or 1). `cleanCode` accepts 4–8 characters.
  - `PEER_PREFIX = 'rss-room-'` (net.js:14).
  - **ICE servers** (`N.ice()`, a promise of `{iceServers}` that both Peers and the network test use): `NET_STUN` (Google and Cloudflare STUN), then the relays (TURN) for players with no direct path between them (strict NAT, carrier NAT on mobile data, Wi-Fi isolation, VPNs, school networks that block UDP). PeerJS's own default relays (`eu-0`/`us-0.turn.peerjs.com`) no longer exist, so without a relay such players can't connect at all. Relays come from `NET_TURN`, entries like `{urls: ['turn:host:80', 'turns:host:443?transport=tcp'], username, credential}`, and from `NET_TURN_URL`, a provider's credentials address answering with such a list (Metered: `https://APP.metered.live/api/v1/turn/credentials?apiKey=KEY`). That one is fetched once a session; each entry is checked (`stun:`/`turn:` URLs, string username and credential; at most 12), and a failed or empty fetch (4 s limit) is tried again on the next call while play goes on with STUN alone. The TCP/TLS entry on port 443 is what gets out of a network that blocks UDP. `NET_TURN` holds the game's own account: Metered's free plan (`standard.relay.metered.ca`, the account's region since it moved from the 500 MB test plan, which used `global.relay.metered.ca`; UDP and TCP on 80 and 443, TLS on 443), 20 GB a month; a region change on Metered's side needs these addresses changed (its dashboard: Get Credential → Show ICE Servers Array), or `NET_TURN_URL` set to its API, which always answers with the current ones; one login for every player, so it is public by nature (it only relays game traffic, and Metered's dashboard can replace it). Chromium's WebRTC doesn't send TURN through an explicit HTTP proxy, so a network that only lets traffic out through one (like the test container) still can't reach the relay; one that filters transparently can. `N.host` and `N.join` wait for `N.ice()` before creating their Peer; `N.leave` bumps `joinGen`, so a room still waiting for its relays doesn't open after Back.
  - **`N.probe()`** (about 10 s): opens a throwaway Peer on the matchmaking server, and gathers ICE candidates from an `RTCPeerConnection` with the same servers. It returns `{server, direct, relay, relaySetUp}`: whether the server answered, whether a `srflx` candidate came (a STUN server answered over UDP), the protocols of the `relay` candidates (`relayProtocol`: udp, tcp or tls) and whether any relay is configured. `MPUI.testNetwork` (the **Test this network** button) turns it into three ✓/✗ rows and a verdict in a modal.
  - `NET_PIECE = 4000`: PeerJS's `json` channel refuses a message of 16300 bytes or more (`message-too-big`, nothing sent). `wire(conn)` sends a message whose JSON is longer than 4000 characters as pieces `{t:'~', k, i, n, s}`, and `unwire()` reassembles them per connection (at most `NET_MAX_BYTES` in all, 8 messages open at once; bad pieces are dropped). Every p2p send and receive goes through them; `N._t` exposes them (and `HOST_ONLY`) for the tests. One message is open per connection at a time (pieces never interleave on the ordered channel), so a link holds at most `NET_MAX_BYTES` of pieces; pieces that rebuild a piece are dropped, and the host closes a connection that sends pieces before its `hello`. A `message-too-big` error no longer drops a player or closes the room.
- **Transports** (`N.transport`):
  - `'p2p'`: PeerJS on the public PeerJS cloud. The host registers peer id `rss-room-CODE` and retries with a new code up to 3 times on `unavailable-id` while the room is opening (never once it is live: that restarted a live room under a new code and dropped everyone). Connections are `{reliable: true, serialization: 'json'}`.
  - **The matchmaking server lost** (`serverLost`/`serverBack`, PR #44). It only lets new players in: the room runs on the direct links. When the host's Peer reports `network`, `server-error`, `socket-error`, `socket-closed` or `unavailable-id`, or `disconnected`, the host sets `N.offline`, says so once (a `warn`), and reconnects quietly after 2 s, then 4, 8, 16, and every 30 s (a failed try sets up the next). Its `open` again ends it (`serverBack`: one `warn` with `ok`, a plain toast). A link that keeps dropping is told at most every 5 minutes, and its waits start over only after it has held for a minute. Before PR #44 every failed try (1.5 s apart) raised the warning again: on `main`, 10 minutes off the server gave 334 notices (`test/net.test.js`). The room card's label reads "Joining paused: reconnecting…" while `N.offline`. A try that finds the link open after all ends it too, and one still connecting is looked at again later, so joining is never left paused. An `open` from a room already left (a reconnect that was on its way) is ignored (`gen`), where it used to start the room's timers again.
  - A player (not the host) in the room ignores its own Peer's errors: its link to the host is direct and says itself when the host goes (`close`, `error`, or 25 s of silence: `watch`). Before PR #44 it warned on each (noise), and left the room on `peer-unavailable`, which the server also answers when the *host's* link to it has dropped while the room plays on.
  - `'local'`: a `BroadcastChannel('rss-room-'+CODE)` carrying envelopes `{to:'H'|cid, from:cid, msg}`. Clients pick a random `cid`.
- **Star topology:**
  - The host is pid 1; clients get pid 2, 3, … from `accept()` (net.js:170).
  - `N.send(msg, to?)` (net.js:107) stamps `msg.f = N.me`. Clients send only to the host. The host's `route()` (net.js:115) forwards to `to`, or to everyone except the sender, and emits locally.
  - **The host overwrites `msg.f` with the real sender's pid** (net.js:189).
  - The host never relays client messages whose type is in `HOST_ONLY` = `{~, hello, welcome, reject, ping, closed, join, leave, warn, state, result, obj, spot}` (net.js:39; `obj` and `spot`, a ground battle's zones and tickets and a team's spots, since PR #38). `~` (a piece) is there because a player's piece relayed whole would be taken by the others as part of the host's own stream, and so could fake anything from the host. Clients ignore those types unless `f === 1` (net.js:268). `result` is listed but never sent.
- **Handshake:**
  1. Client sends `hello {name, ver}`.
  2. Host replies `welcome {pid, code, names}` or `reject {reason}`: wrong version, room locked (`N.lock`, set during PvP phases), or full.
  3. The host emits a local `join`.
- **Limits:** `maxPlayers` is 5 for sandbox and 6 for PvP (mp.js:85). `MP.join` waits up to 8 s for the first `state` after the welcome.
- **Joining online, by stage** (`N.join`; `status(text)` reports each stage to `#mp-status` through `MP.join` and `MPUI`'s `go`):
  1. The Peer opens on the matchmaking server: 15 s, else "Couldn't reach the matchmaking server".
  2. `peer.connect` sends the OFFER. A wrong code, a destroyed host, or a host whose page hasn't run for over about 100 s (the server dropped its id; it takes it back within a couple of seconds of returning) answers `peer-unavailable` within about a second: "No room with that code right now…", which says the same code works once the host is back. Otherwise the room exists, and the client waits **up to 60 s** for the host's answer (the connection's signalling state reaching `stable`, or ICE leaving `new` through `iceStateChanged`), saying after 8 s that the host's game isn't answering. A phone host's page doesn't run while its screen is off or another app is open, but the server keeps its id for about 100 s and holds the OFFER in its socket, so this is the common case: the join goes through as soon as the host comes back.
  3. ICE: up to 20 s from the host's answer, or until ICE `failed`: "Found the host, but couldn't connect to their device" (no direct path and no relay). ICE can stay `new` for good when there's no usable candidate at all, which is why the answer, not ICE, starts this stage.
  4. The connection opens and `hello` goes out: 15 s for the `welcome` or `reject`.
  Before the welcome, a connection `close` fails the join at once. `N.leave` cancels a join under way (`pendingJoin`), so Back on the connect screen leaves nothing armed; `MPUI`'s `go` ends a session only for the attempt that is still current. While a join is looking for the host, the Join button reads **Cancel**, and Enter with a corrected code starts over.
- **Staying reachable:** `MP.awake` holds a screen Wake Lock while in a room (taken again when the game comes back on screen), and on a touch device the online host's lobby says, first thing in its card, to keep the game on screen while friends join (`MPUI.stayHint`). When the page becomes visible with the Peer disconnected from the server, net.js reconnects at once rather than waiting for the 1.5 s timer.
- **Pings and clock:**
  - Every 2 s the host sends `ping {ts, rtt}` to each link. The client replies `pong {ts}`, and the host stores `N.pings.set(pid, rtt)`.
  - The client estimates `hostOffset` as the median of the last 7 samples of `ts + rtt/2 − now`.
  - `N.hostNow()` = `performance.now() + hostOffset`. Use it for every shared deadline (`s.ends`, `th`, `battleAt`).
- **Timeouts:**
  - The host drops a link silent for 20 s.
  - A client closes after 25 s without hearing from the host (`watch` net.js:340).
  - `pagehide` calls `N.leave()`. Leaving sends `bye` (client) or `closed` to everyone (host), then destroys the connection after 300 ms.
- **Events:** `N.on(type, fn)` (net.js:46). Each handler runs inside try/catch (net.js:51), so a throwing handler only logs. `join`, `leave` and `warn` are emitted locally only.

---

### 3. mp.js: session, match, shared world

**Constants** (mp.js:13-28):
- `MP_FORMATS`: `1v1` (n 2), `2v2` (4), `3v3` (6), `ffa` (6, teams 0).
- `MP_DEFAULTS`: format 1v1, build 180 s, test on, polish 120 s, budget 75000, carriers on, friendly fire off, time of day `day`, limit 900 s.
- `MP_CARRIER`: bonus ×1.3, span 13 m, length 16 m, mass 12000 kg.
- `MP_KINDS_PVP`: plane, heli, car (an air battle; a ground battle takes Armour and cars, aircraft only at a respawn with air support: `mpAir`, planes, helicopters and, since the big update, drones flown alone). A sandbox takes any kind; a drone starts at the helipad.
- Ground battles (PR #38, mp.js:33-46): `MP_GROUND` (the ground settings' defaults), `MP_PADS` (the two pad spawns), `MP_SET_OK` (every setting's allowed values, air battles' too: anything else is refused), `MP_WRECKS` 8, `MP_WRECK_MS` 90 s, `MP_WRECK_AGL` 10 m, `MP_ARMS`. Chapter 8 §9.
- Rates: snapshot 15 Hz, part states (`pv`) 200 ms, health (`hp`) 500 ms.
- `MP_MAX_PARTS = 600`: the part limit, on unless the host sets `settings.cap` false (`MP_SET_OK.cap`; never in `MP_DEFAULTS`, so a room that doesn't touch it sends the same state as before, and `mp-air` is unchanged). Off: `MP.maxParts()` is `MP_NET_PARTS` (20000, a forged list's ceiling), and `designProblems` checks the spawn's JSON (`mpSpawnChars`) against `MP_SPAWN_CHARS` (250,000 characters, under `NET_MAX_BYTES`: the biggest craft that passes, 5,339 fuselages, sends 245,672 bytes). The host sets it in a sandbox at any time (it's for the next craft flown: ghosts already built stay, so a craft over 600 parts flown with it off stays on the screens that have it when it comes back on, while a player joining after that won't see it) and in a battle's lobby; `MPUI.partCap` draws the switch in the sandbox card and both battles' settings.
- Team names: Faroes / Shetland.

**Session model:**
- The host's `MP.state` is the truth. `push()` (mp.js:258) sends the whole object as `state` and applies it locally. Clients deep-copy it in `applyState` (mp.js:274) and accept it only from `f===1` (mp.js:176).
- State shape:
  - `{mode, phase, ends, endsLen, round, result, settings, players[], feed[], t0, t0At, tod, battleAt, sides, format}`
  - each player: `{pid, name, team, ready, spawn, alive, kills, deaths, dmg, ping, kind, flying}`, plus in a ground battle `caps, score, sorties, respawnAt, slot, slots, airSlots, troops, cost, sortieEnd`; the state also holds `obj` (GroundMode's tickets and zones)
- Clients change only their own row through `set {k, v}`, validated per key in `applySet` (mp.js:227). The allowed keys are `ready`, `spawn` (must be in `spawnsFor`), `kind`, `flying`, `team` (lobby only), `name` (≤18 characters) and `dmg` (clamped to 0..20000 per report), and in a ground battle `slot`, `troops` and `airSlots`; in a ground battle under way the host ignores `flying`, `ready` and `spawn` from clients (it decides who is on the field from spawns and downs).
- Only the host changes settings (`setSetting` mp.js:246, lobby only). Switching format re-deals teams: in FFA, `team = pid`.
- While in a room, `pushCareer`/`popCareer` (mp.js:135-154) swap out the player's career and saved blueprint, and `Builder.save` is suppressed (builder.js:358). `setMoney` turns the budget into career funds.

**Modes and phases:**
- `sandbox`: phase `free` for the whole session. Anyone can hurt anyone. Respawn is allowed (`respawn` mp.js:556). Everyone shares a clock: `sandboxTime()` = `t0 + elapsed host time`.
- `pvp` runs these phases, with timers set by `setPhase` (mp.js:335) and advanced from `tick()` (250 ms `setInterval`, so it keeps going in background tabs):
  1. `lobby` → `startMatch` (mp.js:326)
  2. `build` (`settings.build` s)
  3. `test` (60 s, solo flight, only if `settings.test`; with test off, build goes straight to battle)
  4. `polish` (`settings.polish` s, only after a test phase)
  5. `battle` (`settings.limit` s)
  6. `results` → `backToLobby` (mp.js:402)
- When everyone is ready in `build` or `polish`, `ends` is set to 3 s from now; it is restored if someone un-readies (`maybeAllReady` mp.js:347).
- **Win** (`checkWin` mp.js:480; a ground battle goes to `checkGround` :577 instead, on tickets, a wiped-out team or time, chapter 8 §9): the battle ends 3.5 s after at most one team (in FFA, team = pid) is still alive, unless only one side started (a solo try-out). On time (`finish` mp.js:390), the winner is the side with the most players still flying, then the most kills; otherwise it is a draw.
- `hostile(a, b)` (mp.js:74) decides who can hurt whom: enemies, or teammates with friendly fire on. `enemy(a, b)` (mp.js:79) decides what can be locked and tagged; teammates are never enemies.
- Silly flags are off throughout a PvP room, in every phase (`Silly.on` checks `MP.pvp`, util.js:143); sandbox rooms keep them. Time warp is disabled in every room (flight.js:202, flight.js:512), and the flight menu does not pause (`openMpMenu` flight.js:469).

**Budgets and limits:**
- `budgetFor` = `settings.budget`, ×1.3 at a carrier spawn (mp.js:428).
- `designProblems` (mp.js:446) reports:
  - more than `maxParts()` parts (any mode; with the limit on, in a sandbox, it says who can turn it off: a battle's settings are fixed by the time anyone builds), or, with it off, a spawn too big to send;
  - over budget;
  - a kind other than plane, heli or car;
  - for carriers: not a plane or heli, span over 13 m, length over 16 m, or mass over 12 t.
- `measure` (mp.js:435): span is the extent of `partZs` ± half-depth (twins ±`max(w,.6)/2`); length is the y extent; mass is the sum of `partKg`.
- `battleDesign` (mp.js:650) swaps a failing design for the first stock template (`mustang`, `camel`, `skylark`) that passes; in a ground battle (PR #38) for the money tier's stock vehicles (`GroundMode.stock`), then the technical, never a plane. A ground battle's spawns are its two pads (`spawnsFor`), with no money bonus or size limits, and the host prices each new craft from its spawn rows for the tickets it costs (`priceSpawn`).
- These checks run **only on the owner's machine**. Peers never re-check budgets.
- The look-alike jets (PR #36) are priced against the lobby's money choices ($45k / $75k / $120k): every one fits $120k as it comes, the MyG-21 $45k and the FS-16 $75k (chapter 1 §5). Only the MyG-21 meets the carrier limits; the others weigh over 12 t (`mp-measure.json` holds each template's span, length, height and mass).

**Spawns:**
- `MP_SPAWNS` (mp.js:21):
  - `faroes`: team 0, the space-centre runway
  - `shetland`: team 1, airfield
  - `scotland`: FFA only (team −1), airfield
  - `carrierA` (west): team 0
  - `carrierB` (east): team 1
  - `carrierC` (south): FFA only
  - carriers appear only if `settings.carriers`.
- Airfield and carrier data: space.js:88-97.
- `spawnsFor` and `defaultSpawn` (mp.js:411-426): in FFA, the default is the base fewest players picked.
- `placement` (mp.js:571): the slot is the player's index among teammates at the same spawn, sorted by pid.
  - Sandbox: offset 220 m along the runway, or ±35 m steps at the helipad.
  - `airfieldPlacement` (mp.js:941): planes at the threshold, 230 m apart.
  - `carrierPlacement` (mp.js:948): two bow catapults at ±6 m, queue 40 m back; helis on the aft deck. `catapult: {length, speed: 78}` is armed for planes only (flight.js:56); press L to launch.

**Our craft → the others** (`sendSpawn` mp.js:591, `afterUpdate` mp.js:609):
- **Spawn rows** `[id, type, x, y, rot, flip, z, att, skin, mm?]` (the tenth column, an armoured part's thickness, since PR #38):
  - x, y and z are rounded to 3 decimals; z is `partZ(p)`, which is the positive spread for twins;
  - `att` is the host part's id, or 0;
  - `skin` is the paint id, or 0.
