| `Radar.has(g)` | mp.js `sees`, mpui.js `spotted` | Other players seen past eye range |
| `innerRadar(d)` | combat.js `destroyPart` (slot 1), `stealthOf`, the smoke loop; vessel.js; flight.js `breakParts`; builder.js; parts3d.js | Everything special about a set inside a nose |

### 1. The data (parts.js)

`radarDef(o)` (parts.js:63) freezes a radar's numbers over the pulse-Doppler defaults; the comment above it (:52-62) explains every field. `innerRadar(d)` (:64) = `d.inner && d.radarSet`: a set inside a body. No part from before the update satisfies it, which is what every gate keys on. `RADAR_SET` (:68) is the inner sets' shared fields: `cat: 'radar'`, `tray: 'Inside a nose'`, `inner`, `flush` (it may overlap its host), `shape: 'radarset'`, `maxTemp` 2400; each set is as deep as it is wide (`depth`), weighs 5 or 10 kg and has its own `hp`.

| Part (key) | kind | range | cone ± | scan s | look-down / notch m/s | lock s | guide | targets | eccm | lpi | other | $ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ranging Radar (`radarRanging`) | RNG | 8 km | 20° | 0.6 | no | – | 0 (guns: LEAD to 3 km, cues heat seekers) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 120 hp | 1,000 |
| Pulse Radar (`radarPulse`) | PULSE | 14 km | 45° | 2.4 | no | 1.8 | 1 (semi-active) | 1 | 0 | 0 | 160 hp | 2,500 |
| Pulse-Doppler Radar (`radarPD`) | PD | 18 km | 60° | 2.0 | yes / 40 | 1.4 | 2 (and a data link) | 2 | 0 | 0 | 180 hp | 3,500 |
| PESA Radar (`radarPESA`) | PESA | 24 km | 60° | 1.2 | yes / 25 | 1.0 | 2 | 4 | 0.5 | 0 | 200 hp | 6,500 |
| AESA Radar (`radarAESA`) | AESA | 26 km | 70° | 0.8 | yes / 15 | 0.7 | 2 | 6 | 0.7 | 0.6 | 200 hp | 9,000 |
| Tail Warning Radar (`radarTail`) | TAIL | 4 km | 60° (behind) | 1.0 | yes / 0 | – | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `warn`: CHECK SIX within 3 km; 100 hp | 1,200 |
| Radar Pod (`radarPod`) | POD | 12 km | 45° | 2.0 | yes / 40 | 1.6 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | a `pod` on a pylon (spoils stealth like a store); ECM shape, `style: 'radar'` | 4,000 |
| Mast Radar (`radarMast`) | MAST | 9 km | all round | 1.5 | yes / 10 | 1.0 | 2 | 2 | 0.3 | 0 | `ground: 8000` (marks vehicles), shape `mastdome` | 6,000 |
| Radar Rotodome (`rotodome`, an old part) | AEW | 45 km | all round | 4.8 | yes / 30 | 2.0 | 2 | 4 | 0.5 | 0 | moved from Fuselage to the Radar tab, tray "Outside" | 12,000 |

- The Radar tab is `PART_CATEGORIES`' last entry (`{ id: 'radar' }`, after the ground update's seven, so the ground tabs stay `slice(13, 21)`), and `VEHICLE_KINDS.plane.cats` and `heli.cats` end in `'radar'`. Its tray groups come from `def.tray` (chapter 1 §7).
- **Active Radar Missiles** (`radarMissilesA`, Weapons, $19,000): the Radar Missiles' numbers plus `weapon.active: 6000`, label ARH, art style `arh` (a grey radome tip and a cyan band). The three internal radar-missile bays (`us5Bay22`, `us5Bay35`, `ruBay57`) gained `active: 6000` too.
- **What the old parts lost:** `radar:` is gone from every part (radar.test.js A2 checks no part has a number for it). The fourteen noses that had one (`cockpitJet`, `cockpitTandem`, `cockpitStealth`, the Radome and the ten look-alike jets' noses) carry `radarFit` instead, the set **Fit a radar** puts back (a pulse-Doppler for most; a pulse radar for the tandem cockpit, the MyG-21's and the FS-4's noses; the PESA for the Sugoi-35's; the AESA for the FS-22's, FS-35's and Sugoi-57's), and their descriptions end "Room inside for a radar set (Radar tab)". The FS-15's nose is "FS-15 Nose" (key unchanged).
- **Art:** `radarset` and `mastdome` are lathe shapes (`LATHE_SHAPES`; parts3d's `LATHE_KIND.radarset` is metal), so 2D = 3D. A set is an antenna face over the front 28 % (a grey dish, rows of slots for the PESA, a gold 5 × 4 tile grid for the AESA, rimmed in `#5fd7ff`) on an electronics drum; in X-ray a 1.5 px cyan outline. `radarset` is in `NO_SKIN` (a set never takes its host's paint). The pod is the ECM capsule with a white radome over its front fifth and no emitter windows or blades (same outline, same clash shape).

### 2. Where a set sits (parts.js, pure)

Beside the clash shapes (parts.js:1614-1705), headless-testable:
- **`radarHost(d)`**: a body a set may sit in: not a wing or tail surface, fin, pod, wheel, leg, weapon, engine, pair, flush or inner part, separator, chute, radar or armour part.
- **`insideShape(d, flip)`**: a host's room, its real outline's span every centimetre along its own axis (`partOutline`, not the clash shape: a `CLASH_BOXED` nose clashes as a box but holds a set only inside its outline), round for a lathe shape, else as deep as it is tall at that station but no deeper than the part. Cached per type and side (`INSIDE_SHAPES`).
- **`partLocal(H, x, y)`**: a point into H's unturned frame. **`insideOf(q, H, m = 0.04)`**: set q wholly inside H, m clear of its skin, tested every 2 cm along H's axis and at both ends of the set.
- **`insideSpot(type, rot, H, want, others, fwd)`**: the first pose inside H for a set: stations 2 cm apart from H's end that faces `fwd` (the craft's nose, `PILOT[kind].F`; its negative for a tail set), each on the outline's middle line, inside and clashing with none of `others` (a set never goes into an internal gun, a gear well or another set). `want` null (a tap): the front-most; `want {x, y}` (a drag): the accepted station nearest it along H's axis, within 1 m.
- **`insideHostOf(s, parts)`**: the host of a set placed without `att` (a design code): the hosts whose rect and depth hold its centre; the first `insideOf(s, h, -0.02)` accepts, else the smallest rect.
- **Joins:** a set joins only the body it sits in, never by its faces: `buildAdjacency` skips sets in its face pass and links a set with no `att` to `insideHostOf` (:1777-1785); `partsFromDesign` writes that host into `att` (never closing a forged loop, :1824).
- **The builder's numbers:** `radarSummary(parts)` (the stats row: the best working forward or all-round radar, a tail set, radar missiles, active ones, a guiding radar), `radarSpecs(R)` (the part card's lines: reach and cover, against a stealth jet at 0.32 of it, look-down and the notch gate, lock time and on a jammer, what it guides, ground marking, jamming resistance, the LPI reach) and `radarProblems(parts)` (the warnings, in order: radar missiles with no guiding radar; a set outside any body; active missiles from a radar without a data link; two radars looking forward). `radarWorks` there, like radar.js's `works`, reads `hp` with the `null` rule (CLAUDE.md: `null <= 0` is true).

### 3. Physics, stealth and damage (vessel.js, combat.js, flight.js)

A set is mass and nothing else to the airframe, and every gate is keyed on `innerRadar`, so no old design changes:
- vessel.js: a set never becomes the nose (`noseAero`/`noseSharp`'s top part, :333), has no frontal or side area (`computeAreas`, :365) and no ground-contact corners (:402). Its mass, the centre of mass, inertia, the part list and joins, hit boxes and blast damage are the only things it changes: that is why the twelve radar jets' baselines moved (fingerprint `edges kept of mass com I reach`, traces `T parts`, handling, combat `boxes blast`, mp-measure `mass`, the Falcon's and FS-16's take-offs) and nothing else did.
- **`stealthOf`** (combat.js:285) skips sets (they sit inside a nose); a Radar Pod counts as a store hung outside (−0.12).
- **`destroyPart` slot 1** (combat.js:88): a set at 0 hp is `broken` and stays where it is, inside its nose: no explosion, no debris, the craft flies on. The player hears "‹name› knocked out: radar out" once, held in the hit's one toast (`Death.lose`) when a burst takes several parts. Its health is saved as any part's (`hp`); a restored flight with a set at 0 reads it as out (no new save key).
- Damage shrinks a radar's reach: `rk(p)` (radar.js:52) is 1 down to 60 % health, then 0.4 + the fraction left (a set at half health reaches 90 %).
- The damage smoke loop skips sets before `Death.partFX` (combat.js:1477): a set inside a nose neither smokes nor burns.
- **`Flight.breakParts`** takes the sets inside a part that blew up with it, unseen (after `Death.impact` and the explosion loop, flight.js:432): no flash of their own, no lone debris. `ripParts` already carries a set in its nose's falling piece. External radars (the pod, the mast, the Rotodome) are torn off as any part.

### 4. A craft's radars and what they see (radar.js)

- **`radars(v)`** (:40): `[{ p, R, ax, ay, cos }]` for v's radar parts, the axis from `partAxis` and the cone's cosine (−2 for all round), cached on `v._rd` by part count. `works(p)` (not broken, health not at 0), `rk(p)` (above), `aboard(v)`.
- **`mainRadar(v)`** (:55): the working radar that guides anything (`guide ≥ 1`) with the longest reach after damage; null with the radar off. It locks, guides and names the lock time (`lockTime`: `lock × (2 − eccm)` on a jammer; 1.4 s, doubled, with none) and the targets at once (`busy`: the missiles it guides, `guided`, already at `tws` targets). `guideRange` is its reach; `radarOf` the longest reach of any working radar but a tail set (the scope's scale, the brackets); `bestRadar` names the scope and the pill.
- **`sees(L, t, tp, job, memo, keep)`** (:117) → `true | 'notch' | 'clutter' | false`. Nothing without a radar part, with the radar off, for a dead launcher or a dead target, and never a drone (the counter-drone kit's job: radar owns that line). `job` picks the radars: `'guide'` those that guide, `'scan'` every working one but a tail set, `'warn'` the tail set, `'paint'` as scan but ignoring the clutter and an LPI radar beyond its quiet reach (what a warning receiver hears). For each radar: inside its cone about its own axis (`L.axis(ax, ay, 0)`); within `Combat.radarReach(range · rk, t, d, eccm, stealth)` (`range × (1 − stealth)`, then against a jammer beyond 5 km `× (0.7 + 0.3·eccm)`, at least 5 km); the ground not in the way (`seesOver`); then `Combat.clutter(L, t, tp, …, R)`: a target 50 m below the radar or under 300 m over the ground is `'clutter'` to a radar that can't look down at any speed, and `'notch'` to one that can if it moves along the beam slower than the radar's `notch` gate (40 m/s for the old rule, as before). One radar seeing it clearly is enough.
- **`seesOver(L, p, d, memo, keep)`** (:104): free when both ends are more than `LOS_TOP` (2 km, over terrain.js's highest ground of 1,515 m) up, less the chord's sag; else `Combat.clearLine(L, p, d, 400)`, the ground and sea sampled every 400 m (at most 40 samples; the frigate's and SAMs' every 150 m), the answer kept on the memo (the missile, a contact) for `keep` s.
- **Stealth** is read through a 0.5 s memo (`stealth(o)`, sim time, deterministic) so a scan of many contacts doesn't walk every part list each time.
- **Eyes** (`eye(v, o, d)`, :149): an aircraft within `6000 × (1 − ½·stealth)` m; a range target drone within 4 km; other range targets always (they keep their brackets). `seen` = eye or the last scan held it (`has`).

### 5. The picture, four times a second (`Radar.step`, `scan`)

`Radar.S` (:15) holds the player's picture; `reset()` clears it. `scan(v, F)` (:232), every 0.25 s while a radar is aboard and on (`S.rcN` counts scans: 0 for an old design, which `radar.test.js` G checks over 10 s of frames):
- **The air:** bandits (`Combat.isFighter`, not down, under control, not landed), the range's target drones, and in a room the other players' craft in the air (not ground kinds, not drones, not hidden). Within the longest reach, `sees(…, 'scan', memo, 0.5)` true → `S.set` and a contact `{ ref, x, y, z, vx, vy, vz, d, k: 'air', side, t }`. A foe the scan doesn't see but a tail set does → `k: 'six'`; any foe a working tail set sees within 3 km, held by another radar or not (a mast, the Rotodome), sets `S.six` (CHECK SIX).
- **The ground** (a radar with `ground`: the mast): the ground AI's vehicles, other players' ground vehicles and the range's ground targets in its cover and in sight → `S.gset`, `k: 'ground'`. Ours only, never shared.
- `side`: a bandit `foe` (an ally `friend`), a player by `MP.enemy` in a battle (everyone `neutral` in a sandbox), a range target `neutral`.
- `S.rc`: what was seen now plus what was seen in the last 1.5 s and is still alive (fading on the scope), nearest first, at most 16. A new foe chirps (1600 Hz twice, at most once a second, not when four or more are on the scope).
- **Solo radar locks on us** (`lockers`, :192, 4 Hz; none in a room, where the others report theirs with `lock`): bandits within `6000 × (1 − stealth)` m with us within 60° of their nose, the frigate while its SAM homes on us, and the ground AI's SPAA whose tracking radar holds us (`Aim.lockTarget`, behind `S.gai`). Combat's `lockers()` (combat.js:1682) adds `Radar.S.locks` to the room's, so the RWR's RADAR LOCK, its tone and its rim strobes work solo too (handbook §7's old loose end).
- **Search strobes** (`scanners`, :291, 2 Hz, an Advanced RWR only): enemy players whose radar paints us (`sees(g, v, tp, 'paint')`, not with their radar off: mp.js's `P.radarOff`), bandits within 16 km less our stealth with us in their nose and in sight, the frigate in Hostile with us in its reach and sight, the AI's search radars in reach and sight. Each new one ticks once (500 Hz).
- **CHECK SIX** (`S.six`): a 900 Hz beep a second unless the missile warning sounds.

### 6. Locks, the pick, launch and guidance (combat.js)

- **`rangeOf(v, w)`** (combat.js:282): a radar missile `min(range, Radar.guideRange(v))`, so **0 without a guiding radar: no lock, and no 5 km fallback**; a heat seeker `range × (1 + irstOf(v))` as before.
- **The lock** (`playerFrame`, :549-582): a radar missile needs `Radar.sees(v, t, tp, 'guide') === true`. A held lock is kept while the radar holds it and `chaffBreaks` doesn't fire; lost (notching, out of reach or the cone, the ground, chaff), it says LOST on the scope for a second with a low tone (`Radar.lost`). The pick (`Radar.S.pick`) wins over "nearest the nose" while it is inside the seeker's cone. Lock time: a radar missile the main radar's `lockTime`, a heat seeker 0.6 s when a radar holds the target (`has`: the radar cues it), else 0.9 s.
- **Firing** (:584-589): no main radar: the missile goes, unguided, with one toast a flight ("No radar to guide the … : they fly straight"); a lock on a new target while the radar already guides missiles at `tws` targets: refused ("Radar busy: the … guides missiles at 2 targets at a time").
- **`launchMissile`** (:405): a craft's radar missile gets `s.act = w.active || 0` and `s.link` (its radar has `guide ≥ 2`).
- **Toasts after death:** `hurtPart`'s "damaged" toasts and a set's "knocked out: radar out" are not said once `sim.destroyed` (the end strip is the message).
- **`guideAir`** (:971): a semi-active missile asks its launcher every step: a craft's through `Radar.sees(s.launcher, t, tp, 'guide', s)` (the frigate's `siteSees`, a ground launcher's `Aim.radarSees`, as before); a notch has 0.4 s of grace and a lost lock 1 s of memory. **Active** (`s.act && s.link`): inside `act` of its target it goes on its own seeker (`s.pit = true`, never back): from then it asks only `clutter` from its own position (a low target crossing its beam can still hide; chaff still works), so the launcher may turn away. From a pulse radar or a pod (no data link) it rides the radar all the way. A missile gone active frees its radar's target slot (`guided` counts `!s.pit`).
- **The warning's ACTIVE:** an active missile homing on us reads `MISSILE ACTIVE` on the Advanced RWR's headline, `A` on its scope, and "ACTIVE: chaff now · turn across its path" as advice (`rwrAdvice`).
- **The controller's call** (`call`/`clock`, :389-401): as each solo bandit spawns in Hostile, "Bandit inbound: 14 km, your 10 o'clock" (the clock from the nose in the local horizontal; straight up or down, the belly's way).
- **LPI:** a lock on another player is reported (`MP.sendLock`) only while `Radar.heard(v, t, was)`: an AESA's (lpi 0.6) within 10.4 km, and once reported held to 3 % past it (`HEARD_H`, `was`: the lock last sent was this one), so a craft sitting on the edge doesn't flicker RADAR LOCK on and off.
- **The pick** goes once it is no target (`pickable`, every frame at the top of `step`): dead, a bandit shot down and falling (`ai.down`), anyone's falling hulk (`down`), a wreck. Left on a falling hulk it would draw its white square on the wreck and keep the next lock off the held-lock path (no chaff break, no LOST).
- **A down craft's radar is read by nothing:** `sees` refuses `L.down` (a pilot knocked out leaves the set aboard and whole), so its semi-active missiles go dumb a second later, as when the set is shot out.
- **The 3D-view markers** (`drawHUD`): range targets bracketed out to `clamp(radarOf × 0.7, 14 km, 32 km)` (a target drone only once `seen`); a bandit as a diamond within eye range, a hollow square when only the radar holds it, the pick white with ▸; each marker registered for taps (`Radar.mark`); the mast's ground contacts as small amber squares with their distance; LOCK with its distance; the gun's LEAD on the nearest aircraft within 2 km, or 3 km when the radar holds it (the pick first), and `S.gunIn` (the radar holds the gun's target inside 900 m) turns the pipper, or Aim's cross (`drawFlyAim`), yellow.

### 7. The scope and the RWR (combat.js `drawRWR`, `rwrPlace`, radar.js `draw`)

- With a radar part aboard (`Radar.aboard`, not aiming a ground vehicle), `drawRWR` draws the radar's scope where the Advanced RWR's would go, and the RWR's marks (`rwrMarks`: search strobes as faint rim ticks, locks as red rim strobes, missiles as red triangles with `A`, `IR`, `R` or `M`) go on the same disc. An old design without a radar draws exactly what it drew before. A tail set says CHECK SIX ‹distance› (amber) even without a warning receiver.
- **`rwrPlace(W, H, rad)`** (:1790) gives the radar's disc a bigger radius than the RWR's 40-46 px, by the screen and the HUD's size (40 px on a screen under 500 px tall, 52 under 500 px wide, 54 under 761, else `64 × clamp(Controls.z, 0.85, 1.3)`) and also keeps it clear of the buttons top right (`.flight-top-right`), the time warp (`#warp`), the hint (`#hint`) and the flight controls round it (BRAKE, the throttle, the stage box, the autopilot bar, flaps and gear, the weapon box, RDR, FIRE, CM), besides everything the RWR's scope keeps clear of. Its label is kept clear too, at its widest (32 letters with `km`, 29 without, centred and clamped to the screen as `draw` puts it): on one line where any spot has room for it, else on two (`S.rwrBox.lines` 2: `draw` puts the `DMG` part under the rest, 22 letters at most), the spots beside the flight data and the left controls moved out by the label's half width. `S.rwrBox.rad` says which it placed.
- **`Radar.draw(ctx, B, v, F, at, o)`** (radar.js:416), heading up with us in the middle, at most 60 canvas calls with ten contacts and a pick (`radar.mjs` counts them): the disc and rim (red while warned or locked), a half ring and a cross; each working radar's cover as a lit wedge about its axis's bearing (three at most; all round a disc); the main radar's sweep (a bar across a forward radar's cone, a turn for an all-round one, at its `scan` time); the contacts as squares (hollow amber for the ground), one path per colour and age (fresh, or fading), each with a tick along its heading; the pick (a white square) and the lock (a green diamond); our radar missiles (white dots, ringed once active); with a radar missile chosen, its reach as a dashed arc across the main radar's cover; and a label (`PD 18 km · SRCH`, `LOCK 9.1`, `GUIDE 2`, `BUSY`, `LOST`, `OFF`, amber `DMG 40%`, red `RADAR OUT`). The range scale is a square root out to the longest working reach rounded up to 5 km, so near things spread out.
- The room's own 8 km scope (`MPUI.drawRadar`, wide screens) is separate and unchanged.

### 8. Commands and the HUD (flight.js, controls.js, main.js)

- **Keys** (controls.js `KEY_ACTIONS`, Weapons group): `radarNext` I (`Shift+I` back) and `radarPower` O, handled in flight.js's keys (:1669-1670). **`next(F, dir)`** (:322) steps the pick through the air candidates seen by eye or radar by angle off the nose; past either end it goes back to auto (nearest the nose); it clears the missile lock and toasts "Target: Bandit 12 km". **`toggle(F)`** (:340): `v.radarOff` (the scope dims, the picture clears, a radar missile's lock drops; in a room `MP.sendRadar`); refused for an inert craft (`v.down`, `F.destroyed`).
- **Taps** (`Radar.tap`, from `Flight.tapAt` before anything else): a tap on the scope steps the pick; a tap within 30 px of an air marker (a bandit, a target drone, an enemy player's tag) picks it.
- **The RDR pill** (`#btn-radar`, touch only: `CTL.radar` in the layout editor, `'air'`; hidden on a desktop and in the ground HUD): a tap is `next`, a 0.6 s hold (its ring filling, timed from the touches' own time stamps) is `toggle`. Its words come from `Radar.pill(v)`: `RDR PD`, `RDR OFF` (dimmed), `RDR OUT` (red). `updateHUD` shows it and the weapon box (even on a craft with nothing to fire) while a radar is aboard (`radarPill`, flight.js:2302); an old craft never touches it.
- **The weapon chip:** `Combat.status` sets `radar` from `Radar.chipState(v, cur)` for an air radar missile: `ok`, `off`, `none` (no radar aboard), `guns` (only radars that guide nothing) or `out` (every radar broken); anything but `ok` puts ⊘ before the name, and `Radar.step` says each state once a flight in a toast.
- **Launch toasts** (main.js `App.launch`): radar missiles with no radar part ("No radar on board: radar missiles can't lock. Fit one in the builder (Radar tab)."), always; a craft's first flight with a radar part, how to use it, once ever (`rss.radarTip`, with hints on).
- Help in three places, written from the bindings: README's table, `App.showHelp`'s Radar item, `Flight.controlsHelp`'s row; and the key hints (`keyHints`) while a radar is aboard. The builder's X-ray key reads "X-ray (armour and radar)".

### 9. The builder (builder.js, parts3d.js, render3d.js)

- **A tap** on a set in the tray (`quickAdd` → `radarSpot(type)`, :1446): it goes into the front of the first nose, cockpit or fuselage on the centre line with room for it (the hosts front first, `radarHosts`; a tail set into the back of the last: `radarFwd(warn)`), turned to face the nose (`defaultRot`); a set already aboard of the same kind (forward or tail) is swapped out, the new one trying its host first (one undo step). It says where it went and peeks (below). No room: "No room for the … inside: try a bigger nose (a Radome has the most room) or a Radar Pod."
- **A drag** (2D `snap`, 3D `attach`): a set sinks into the body drawn on top under the pointer at the station nearest it (`insideSpot` with `want`), or shows red and the drop does nothing (never onto a wing or a fin); a set dragged on moves into another body. **The Rotodome and the Mast Radar** go on top: on a plane on the back of the widest body at the centre of mass, elsewhere on top of the stack. Radar pods hang as pods always did.
- **Fit a radar** (`#s-fix`, `fitRadar`, :1470): while "need a radar" holds, puts in the front-most host's `radarFit` (else a pulse-Doppler).
- **Seeing a set:** aircraft have their own X-ray switch (`xrayAir`, `rss.xrayAir`, off by default; Armour keeps `rss.xray` exactly as before; `xrayArmour`, `xrayOn`, `xrayShown`, `setXray`, :1927-1939): the bodies holding sets fade (0.35 in 2D, 0.3 in 3D) and the sets draw over them in cyan; in 3D a tap there picks the set (parts3d `pick`). **The peek** (`peek`): placing or selecting a set shows X-ray for 1.6 s, then one redraw covers it again (`redraw.mjs` counts it). The host's card has a "Holds: AESA Radar ›" chip (`#pi-holds`) that selects the set; a selected radar draws its cover (a ±cone wedge, a circle for all-round ones, three craft lengths out); a set's depth row is hidden (its depth is its host's).
- **Numbers:** the tray groups ("Inside a nose", "Outside"), each line saying how far and where a radar looks (`specLine`: "26 km ±70°", "4 km behind", "45 km all round"); `radarSpecs` on the part card; the Radar stats row (`#s-radar-row`, `renderRadar`: "AESA 26 km ±70° · tail", "none" in amber when radar missiles have nothing to guide them); `radarProblems` among the warnings for any kind of craft.
- **3D:** a set is `inner`, so parts3d never builds it in flight; in the hangar `xrayRig` (parts3d.js) colours it X-ray cyan and fades the body holding it (`rig.rhost`). The Rotodome turns in flight only (`A.always && !this.builder`; render3d's `bd.anim` is false), so an idle hangar holding one draws nothing.

### 10. Stock jets and prices

The twelve jets that had a nose radar or radar missiles carry their era's set as their **last** row (so no `att` index moves), hosted by their nose, at the spot a tap in the builder gives (within 1 cm): the Falcon, FS-16, MyG-29, FS-15 and Sugoi-27 a pulse-Doppler, the Kestrel and FS-4 a pulse radar, the MyG-21 a ranging set (it cues its heat seekers), the Sugoi-35 a PESA, and the FS-22, FS-35 and Sugoi-57 an AESA. Each nose's price dropped by its set's, so every radar jet costs what it did (the MyG-21 and FS-16 at their battles' caps, the FS-22 at $120k); the Striker, Hornet, Viper and Spectre are $3,500 cheaper (their nose, no set). The Sugoi-35's blurb names its PESA; the Spectre's says it has no radar ("it stays silent"). `radar.test.js` B5-B6 pin the rows and the prices.

### 11. Multiplayer (mp.js, mpui.js)

- **`rdr {on: 0|1}`** (owner → all): `Radar.toggle` sends it (`MP.sendRadar`); while our radar is off it is said again every 5 s (for players who joined since; nothing while it is on), and a spawn sent again to someone who missed it is followed by `rdr` off (their copy of our spawn turned it on). The receiver (mp.js:272) takes only the numbers 0 and 1 from a known peer (forged values and unknown senders are ignored, no peer is made) and keeps `P.radarOff`, which a new spawn resets (:1174). Its only effect: that player's radar paints no search strobes on our Advanced RWR.
- **`mp.a`**: an active radar missile's `mp` rows carry `a: 1` once it is on its own seeker (absent before, so every old row is the old row exactly); `Combat.netMissileUpdate` sets the replica's `pit` on the number 1 only, and it stays set (ACTIVE in the warning).
- **Seeing other players:** an air battle's tags and the room's scope (`MPUI.spotted`, mpui.js:653) keep the 6 km × (1 − ½·stealth) eye rule and past it ask `Radar.has(g)` (our own radar's last scan; nothing with it off, no radar aboard or once destroyed). In a ground battle `MP.sees` (mp.js:1719) counts our radar's **air** contacts for aircraft against aircraft, so a radar plane spots enemy aircraft for its team through the existing `seen`/`spot` messages; a mast's ground contacts stay ours. Each enemy tag drawn is a radar mark (a tap on it picks that player).
- Radar parts ride the spawn rows like any part. `NET_VERSION` stays 11 (the update's one bump); its comment names radar parts, `rdr` and `mp.a`.

### 12. Invariants and gotchas

1. **No magic radar.** Radar numbers live only in `def.radarSet` (radar.test.js A checks no part has a `radar` number and no old part but the Rotodome is a radar). A new way to see past eye range goes through a radar part and `Radar.sees`, never a number on a cockpit.
2. **AI bandits keep their implicit radar** (`flyFighter`'s 16 km, `scanners`' strobes): nothing in radar.js steers or arms them, and `combat.test.js` pins their chases.
3. **Gate on `innerRadar`.** Every special case for a set (vessel.js, `stealthOf`, `destroyPart`, the smoke loop, `breakParts`, the builder) keys on it; no old part satisfies it, so old designs fly, hit and look as before (radar.test.js C stubs it to false and finds every template without a set unchanged).
4. **A set at 0 hp is broken, not gone** (slot 1, before `module`, the drones' warhead line and the air rule). Test it with `works(p)` / `radarWorks(p)`, never `hp <= 0` alone.
5. **Drones are never radar contacts** (`sees`' first lines): the counter-drone kit owns them.
6. **The picture is the player's craft's only**, deterministic (sim time; no `Math.random`, no wall clock in a decision), and empty for an old design (`S.rcN === 0`).
7. **Old messages stay byte for byte:** `rdr` is new, `mp.a` is absent until a missile goes active, and mp-air.json is identical.
8. **The scope stays inside its budget** (60 canvas calls) and inside `rwrPlace`'s box; a new panel on the flight HUD that it must avoid goes into `rwrPlace`'s `extra` list.
9. **The rotodome's hangar spin is off** (battery): anything that animates in the 3D hangar must keep `bd.anim` honest (chapter 3 §7 item 14).

## 10. Drones and counter-drone (drone.js)

The big update's drones: a sixth kind of craft, the **Drone**, built from its own small parts in four tabs and flown by its own laws (never a helicopter's or a plane's), alone from the helipad or from a tank's **drone bay**; and the tanks' answer, **counter-drone kit** (jammers, a detector, a Drone Radar, an anti-drone gun, a laser and a roof cage). The computer flies drones in the **drone war** (the Tank Range and the Practice Battle), and in a room a player's drone flies beside their tank as their **second craft** (the craft index `c` on its messages).

Files: `js/drone.js` (globals `DRONE`, `DRONE_LEGACY`, `Drone`; loaded after groundai.js and before builder.js, SHELL entry after `js/groundai.js`; no top-level DOM or THREE, so it loads headless). Its sections, in order: **core** (tuning, groups, the laws, the battery, the builder's numbers, what fits a bay, building one), **HUD**, **bays**, **combat** (fuses, warheads, the counter-drone kit), **AI** and **MP** (empty: mp.js carries the messages). The rest lives where it always would: the catalogue in parts.js, the physics hooks in vessel.js, the flight's controls, cameras and saves in flight.js, render3d.js and controls.js, the builder in builder.js and parts3d.js, the drone war in groundai.js, the room's half in mp.js, gmode.js, mpui.js and ghud.js. drone.js draws no `Math.random` in the simulation (a part's spread is `prand(p)`, a stream seeded from its id; the AI decides on the drone's own clock `dl.t`), so the tests' runs repeat. Tests: `test/drones.test.js` (the catalogue, the laws, the battery, the link, the bays, payloads, counter-drone, the AI, the builder's numbers, the gates; chapter 6) and `test/browser/drone-flight.mjs` (the HUD on five screens, the bays in the page).

### 0. Where the game calls in, and the gates

**The gates.** Old designs never reach any of it. Every hook outside drone.js opens only for one of: kind `'drone'`, a part with `def.drone`, `def.cuas` or `def.dock`, a `searchRadar` with `low`, `v.carrier` (a drone from a bay), `Combat.S.dr`, `GroundAI.dw`, or a message's `c`/`tc`. `Combat.S.dr` (combat.js `frame`) is true while anything drone-related is in play (the drone war, or a vessel that is a drone or carries counter-drone kit or a bay): it costs one `some()` a frame and is false for every old flight. `test/ground.test.js` counts every call into `Drone` (it is in `SYSTEMS` beside `Gear`, `Aim`, `Armour`, `GroundMode`, `GroundAI` and `Props`) while the old templates fly, fight, save and go through rows, codes and spawns, and checks `Combat.S.dr` never opens: both stay at zero.

| Call | From | When |
|---|---|---|
| `Drone.groups(v)` | vessel.js `rebuildGroups` | Kind `'drone'` only: its controller, links, cameras, cells, lift rotors, guards and warheads (`v.dr`); every other craft `v.dr = null` |
| `Drone.pre(v, dt, sim)` | vessel.js `physicsStep`, before `applyEngines` | A drone that isn't debris, every physics step: the overrides and the throttle (§3) |
| `Drone.turn` / `wingTurn` | vessel.js `turnTarget` | A drone's pilot rates (a multirotor's or a winged drone's law) |
| `Drone.frame(F, dt)` | combat.js `frame`, while `S.dr` | The links, the bays' upkeep, LAST FLIGHT, clean-up, the AI's drones, the beams, the marks |
| `Drone.step(h, F)` | combat.js `step`, while `S.dr` | Every substep: the fuses of drones with warheads, the anti-drone guns and lasers |
| `Drone.cookOff` | combat.js `destroyPart`, slot 3 (after a radar set's and a module's lines, before the air rule) | A warhead at 0 hp bursts as high explosive |
| `Drone.detonate` | combat.js `playerFrame` (`weapon.kind 'warhead'`) | FIRE sets the charge off by hand, armed only |
| `Drone.threats` | combat.js `candidates` | The drones hostile to the seeker's owner are air targets |
| `Drone.alarm` | combat.js `damage`, mp.js `onHit` | Our parked tank hit while its drone flies |
| `Drone.netFire` | combat.js `netGuns` | A ghost's anti-drone gun or laser at work, for show |
| `Drone.ownedAir` | armour.js (proximity shells) | An enemy proximity shell bursts by the player's drones nobody flies |
| `Drone.marked` | radar.js `eye`, mp.js `sees`, mpui.js tags, ghud.js minimap | Who sees a drone (§8) |
| `Drone.key`, `home`, `land`, `lost`, `forSave`, `cleanChoice`, `lagged`, `fov`, `tap`, `help`, `alertText`, `batt`, `linkState`, `leverText`, `left`, `jamN` | flight.js (keys, pad, `onDestroyed`'s first line, `serialize`/`restore`, `readControls`, `setFpv`, `updateHUD`, `droneHUD`) | The bays, the flight's HUD and saves |
| `Drone.drawOSD`, `drawMarks` | main.js, after `Combat.drawHUD` | The FPV display (or the impact view); the marks while `S.dr` |
| `Drone.beams` | render3d.js `updateStreaks` | The lasers' beams, drawn by the streak mesh (no mesh of its own) |
| `Drone.stats`, `fits`, `cleanChoice` | builder.js | The stats, the bay picker, a design's or a code's pick |
| `Drone.spawnAI`, `seeR` | groundai.js | The drone war's drones; how far an AI vehicle picks a drone out |
| `Drone.fits`, `lastFlight`, `spotR`, `alarm`, `marked` | mp.js | A peer's drone spawn; a tank knocked out under its drone; spotting |
| `Drone.left` | controls.js `keyHints` | "U: 3 left" |
| `DRONE.LAUNCH.SCALE`/`ZOOM` | flight.js `naturalScale`, zoom | A drone's view comes closer |

### 1. The data (parts.js)

- **The kind:** `VEHICLE_KINDS.drone` = `{ name: 'Drone', site: 'helipad', launchRot: 0, cats: ['drFrame', 'drPower', 'drLink', 'drPay'] }`. `AIR_KINDS` holds `'drone'` (the damage model's air rule; `isAirKind`), `GROUND_KINDS` doesn't. Coordinates as a helicopter's: +x forward, +y up, +z right.
- **Categories:** the four drone tabs (Frames, Motors & power, Links & sensors, Payloads) sit in `PART_CATEGORIES` after Kit and before Radar, so the ground tabs stay `slice(13, 21)`, the drone tabs are `slice(21, 25)` and Radar is the 26th (index 25).
- **The 35 drone parts** (none `gv`; each part's role is its `def.drone`; the comment above `drFC` explains every field). Masses are in tonnes without the battery's charge; a battery's `fuel` is its charge at **1 kg = 1 kWh**; a motor's `ispSL` is 3600 × its grams of lift per watt, so the old engine code drains watt-hours at the right rate; `cda` is a part's drag area (wings carry their own); `tray` its tray group. `def.drone`: `fc` (a flight controller's limits: `kind` fpv or gps, `lean` rad, `vmax` m/s, `vz` m/s climb, `dive` (an FPV dives steeper the faster it flies), `fs` the failsafe `'drop'` or `'home'`, `radio` its own radio's reach), `frame.tips` (the motor stations, `[x, z]`), `motor`, `guard`, `legs`, `body`, `cell`, `loud` (a generator), `link {kind radio|long|fibre|sat, range, lag}`, `cam {tilt, fov, spot, gimbal, zoom, thermal}`, `warhead {pen, tandem, he, blast, dmg}`, `hp` (its hit points: combat.js `maxHp` reads `def.drone.hp`, one or two near bursts) and `rot` (the turn it goes on at).

| Tab (tray groups) | Parts |
|---|---|
| Frames (13: Controllers, Multirotor, Fixed wing) | FPV Flight Controller (`drFC`: lean 0.75, 30 m/s, falls on a lost link, $200) and GPS Flight Controller (`drFCgps`: lean 0.45, 16 m/s, flies home, $600), both with a 1 km radio; FPV, Quad, Hex and Octo Frames; Landing Legs and Skids; a Prop Guard; the winged munition's Munition Body, Drone Wing, Drone Tail and Drone Fin |
| Motors & power (9: Motors, Batteries) | Racing Motor (1.6 kg of lift, 4.2 g/W), Lift Motor (0.8 kg, 6 g/W), Heavy Motor (6 kg), Ducted Fan (2 kg, 3 g/W, guarded by its ring), Pusher Motor (3.1 kg of thrust, for a winged drone); batteries of 50, 100 and 700 Wh and a 2 kWh Hybrid Generator (`loud`: detectors hear it twice as far) |
| Links & sensors (7: Links, Cameras) | Radio Link (2.5 km), Long-range Link (8 km), Fibre Spool (5 km) and Long Fibre Spool (12 km: unjammable, silent, snap for good past their length), Satellite Link (50 km, a quarter second of lag); FPV Camera (100°, tilted 25° up, spots ground vehicles to 1.5 km) and Gimbal Camera (1×/4×/12× zoom, thermal, 3 km) |
| Payloads (6: Warheads, Drops, Missiles) | Shaped Charge (350 mm), Tandem Charge (650 mm, beats bricks and cages), Frag Charge (no jet, a 7 m burst); Grenade Dropper (three) and Bomb Rack (two 3 kg bombs), with `he`; Mini Missiles (two, 3 km, 450 mm, `seek: 'ground'`, outside every radar rule) |

- **The 10 armour parts** (`def.gv`, `family: 'Drones'`, shown in the tray behind a Drones label after the eras; `PART_ERAS` builder.js):

| Part (key) | Tab | What | $ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drone Rack (`dockRack`) | Launchers | `dock {n: 3, kg: 3.5, span: 0.8, budget: 3000, stock: 'bumble', launch: 'hop'}` | 10,000 |
| Drone Hangar (`dockHangar`) | Launchers | `dock {n: 1, kg: 25, span: 1.8, budget: 9000, stock: 'mavik', launch: 'hop'}` | 12,000 |
| Launch Tubes (`dockTubes`) | Launchers | `dock {n: 2, kg: 8, span: 1.8, budget: 4500, wing: true, stock: 'lancett', launch: 'tube', v0: 28, el: 20}` | 11,000 |
| Drone Jammer (`cdJam`) | Kit | `cuas.jam` 500 m | 4,000 |
| Wide-band Jammer (`cdJamL`) | Kit | `cuas.jam` 1,000 m | 9,000 |
| Drone Detector (`cdDetect`) | Kit | `cuas.detect` 2 km | 2,000 |
| Drone Radar (`cdRadar`) | Kit | `searchRadar {range: 4000, rpm: 90, low: true}` | 8,000 |
| Anti-drone Gun (`cdGun`) | Kit | `cuas.gun {range 600, cued 1100, rate 10, speed 1000, ab 3, dmg 8, ammo 200, …}` | 9,000 |
| Anti-drone Laser (`cdLaser`) | Kit | `cuas.laser {range 1000, cued 1500, kill [0.8, 2.2], on 8, cool 5, …}` | 15,000 |
| Roof Cage (`cdCage`) | Kit | `slat: 0.5, cage: true`, flush over the turret | 1,500 |

- **On a vessel** (vessel.js `rebuildGroups`, for gv craft only): `v.cuas` (counter-drone parts), `v.docks` (bays), `v.cdLow` (a working Drone Radar), and an anti-drone gun's `ammo` (saved as any part's). Old vessels carry the frozen `NO_GEAR` and `false`.
- **Art (2D = 3D).** `drLayout` (parts.js) is one table of each part's pieces (plates, arms, bells, props, whips, domes, lenses, stores, lids, the gun's and laser's heads) that both the 2D side views (`drPath`/`drDraw`, the kit's `drKit`/`drKitPlan` through `gvKit`/`gvPlan`) and parts3d's recipes read: `droneFrame`, `droneMotor` (its prop on a spinning node, `DR_SPIN` 8 times the physics' spin), `droneBox`, `droneLegs`, `droneGuard`, `droneMissile`, `dock` (the rack's folded quads hide one by one as they launch, from `p.docked`), `rws` and `laserT` (yaw and pitch nodes posed from `aimYaw`/`aimEl`) and `cage`; the munition body, charges and spools are lathes. A drone part's texture has 8 times the pixels a metre. Five motors carry `plume: 'none'`. builder-shapes.mjs checks every one of the 41 boxed models within 1.5 cm of its clash box.

### 2. Physics (vessel.js; every line gated on the kind or a new field)

- `PILOT.drone` (the stick's axes), the drone's own drag (`droneCdA`: its parts' `cda` or 0.6 × width × depth, ×(1 + 1.5·sin²) with the air through its props), the motors' full yaw authority (×1, a helicopter's 0.6 with a tail rotor), `turnTarget`'s drone line, a guarded motor surviving a touch under 6 m/s (mirrored in gear.js), and the battery's flame-out at 1e-6 kg (a drone runs to the last milliwatt-hour; every other craft at 0.01).
- A saved drone flies Level or Acro (`restore`: any other `sas` becomes `'hold'`).
- The damage model's drone clauses (death.js, D1-D2's lines): a motor is a main rotor (torn off and falling; "‹name› shot off!"), the controller's knock-out reads SHOT DOWN, never PILOT KO; **no fires on drones** (`ignite` returns false: grams of battery); a crash is a fireball only with a live warhead or bombs aboard, else a thud (`hotOf`); a torn-off motor and a burnt-out controller throw a few sparks and a wisp scaled by the part's size (`clamp(max(w, h) / 2, 0.15, 1)`), and a drone's damage smoke is as small.

### 3. The laws and `DRONE` (drone.js core)

`DRONE` holds every number (angles in rad, speeds in m/s, times in s): the Level law (`KA` 6, `RMAX` 4, `KV` 0.15 rad of lean per m/s of speed error, `KZ` 3, the height hold `HOLD` 1.5 and `VHOLD` 3, `LOOK` 1.2 s ahead, `YAW` 2, `TURNK`, `BANK` 0.5, `SLIDE` and `BACK` 0.5, `LAND` 1.5 m/s within `LANDH` 3 m, `FLOOR` 1.5 m), Acro (`ACRO` [4.5, 4.5, 3], the throttle's `RAMP` 2.25/s, `IDLE` 0.08), a spin-out (`SPIN`), the winged law (`WING`), safe-arm and the fuse (`ARM_D` 20 m, `ARM_T` 2 s, `FUSE_V` 4 m/s, `AHEAD` 0.3 m), the link (`LINK`), the battery (`BATT` 25 %, 10 %, the 5 % auto-land), Home (`HOME`), the launch (`LAUNCH`), and `LAST` 20 s, `LOST_GONE` 60 s, `REST_GONE` 3 s, `EYE` 400 m, `MARKS` 8, `BUZZ` 250 m, `MAX_PARTS` 40, `IMPACT` 1.5 s. `DRONE_LEGACY.droneCore`: the old Drone Core on a drone-kind craft is a GPS controller with a satellite link (its def untouched).

- **`groups(v)`** (:54) → `v.dr`: the fastest controller (`fc`, `fcPart`; none: the GPS defaults), the links (the controller's own radio first, every link part, the Drone Core's satellite link), the best camera (a gimbal first, then the furthest-seeing), the cells and their full charge `wh0`, the lift rotors and `mot0` (how many it was built with, kept across rebuilds), the guarded motors (a guard's box round a motor, or a ducted fan's own ring), the warheads, and `wing` (an aircraft with no lift rotor).
- **`state(v)`** → `v.dl`, the law's state, made on first use: its own clock `t`, the height hold, the ground look-ahead's cache, the heading frame `S` (`hframe`: velocities ahead, right and up, the heading), the mode (`pilot`, `hold`, `home`, `land`), the link (`q`, `lost`, `snapped`, `via`, `dist`, `jam`), safe-arm (`arm`, `at0`, `t0`), a launch's hop, the battery's warnings.
- **`pre(v, dt, sim)`** (:175), every physics step, before the engines: (1) the **overrides**, each zeroing the stick so a key held at a switch flies nothing: a bay launch's hop; a lost link (an FPV controller cuts the throttle and falls; a GPS one flies Home); Home, Land or holding (a winged drone circles instead of holding); the AI's steering; (2) a multirotor short of motors (a quad down one, a hex or octo down two) **spins and sinks**, the stick still working (control first); (3) Acro or a winged drone: the throttle is the pilot's, ramped by the lever, kept off idle in the air; (4) **Level**: the 5 % auto-land (2 s with no input; any input cancels it), sitting on the ground with the props idle, then the climb-rate law: the lever asks for a climb (`z × vz`) or a descent (steeper the faster an FPV flies; `LAND` near the ground), let go it holds the height **over the ground** it was let go at, looking `LOOK` s ahead along its way (the sea's surface over water, a deck on a deck); the throttle is the thrust that gives `G0 + (vzT − vz)·KZ`, over the tilt.
- **`turn(v, c, dt)`** (:240): Acro, the stick's rates (nothing levels it). Level: the stick asks for a **ground speed** ahead (`vmax`, back at half) and aside (the slide), the drone leans for it (no more than its controller's `lean`) and turns at `0.3 + g·tan(TURNK·lean)/v` (capped at `YAW`), carving with a matching bank at speed; let go, it brakes to a hover. Settings → Drone stick: **Turn** (sideways turns, Q/E slide; the default) or **Tilt** (swapped).
- **`wingTurn(v, c, dt)`** (:258), a drone with a wing: bank to turn with the nose following round, forward dives and back climbs, let go and it holds the height it was let go at; under 1.25 × its stall speed the nose goes no higher and the bank no further than 29°. Never the plane laws (`planeTarget`) or their state: a plane's alone.
- **The battery** (`charge`, `battery`): 25 % (a beep, a toast, amber), 10 % (a beep every 2 s, red; a drone left hovering flies home, or lands with no carrier), flat (the motors flame out). Only the pilot's own drones speak.
- **The builder's numbers** (`stats`, :300): mass with the charge and stores, thrust over weight, the battery's Wh, hover power and minutes (the motors' thrust-weighted grams per watt), cruise minutes (0.9 of hover; a winged drone's at 0.6 throttle, where drag equals thrust), Level's top speed (the lean against drag, capped by the controller's `vmax`) and Acro's, the lean it holds, its best link and camera, the bays it fits and why the others refuse it. **`fits(x, D)`** (:352): a drone with a controller, no armour part, at most 40 parts, within the bay's price, mass and span (as `MP.measure` measures it), winged for tubes and a multirotor otherwise, with the picker's words for each refusal. **`cleanChoice(d)`** (:370): a bay's pick from a design, a code or a save, untrusted: `{key}` for a stock drone (`hasOwn(TEMPLATES, …)`, kind drone) or `{name, parts}` (cleaned rows, ≤ 40, a controller, no armour), else null. **`build(rows, name)`** (:390) makes a drone vessel as `Flight.start` would.

### 4. Flying one (flight.js, render3d.js, controls.js, main.js)

- **The start** (`Flight.start`): a drone launches from the helipad (`VEHICLE_KINDS.drone.site`), its motors on, Level (Settings → Drones start in: Acro for a multirotor), `v.dl` with where it took off; a winged drone can't take off, so it starts 120 m up at 26 m/s. The view's scale comes closer (`naturalScale`, capped at `DRONE.LAUNCH.SCALE`; zoom to `LAUNCH.ZOOM`). It starts in the view last picked for drones (Settings → Drone view: first person, first person with a level horizon, or chase).
- **Keys** (controls.js `KEY_ACTIONS`, the **Drones** group, all in `Keys.LATE`): `drone` U (context `bay`: launch from a bay, switch between drone and tank), and in context `'drone'` `dView` H, `dLevel` 1, `dAcro` 2, `dHome` 3, `dLand` 4 and `dThermal` J (the gimbal's thermal; T stays chat). The clash rule treats `'drone'` as it does `'plane'` and `'rocket'`. `Flight.droneKey` routes them; Shift/Ctrl are the climb lever in Level and the throttle (ramped) in Acro and on a winged drone; Z/X do nothing in Level. The three help lists (README's Drones block, `App.showHelp`'s Drones item and section, `Flight.droneHelp`) and `keyHints` are written from the bindings; `bayLine` adds the U line to `groundHelp`/`driveHelp` for a vehicle with a bay.
- **Touch:** the throttle box is a spring-back **climb lever** in Level (`body.drone-level`; ▲ ▼ hold too; its label `Drone.leverText`: `HOLD 34 m`, `▲ m/s 7.2`, `LANDED`, on two short lines); a six-button **mode pad** in the autopilot grid (Level, Acro, the view, Home, Land, Tank for a bay's drone: `syncSas`, `droneSas`); `#btn-drone` (DRONE) on a carrier's ground HUD (`CTL`, GHUD's blocks; hidden on a desktop; never shown with the RDR pill).
- **FPV** (`Flight.setFpv`, render3d's `fpvEye` and the camera's FPV branch, `Render3D.setFpv(on, fov)`): the eye on the camera part's front face, an FPV camera tilted 25° up and rolling with the drone (or the horizon held level), a gimbal looking along the heading, level, turned by a drag, `C` held or the right button, zoomed 1×, 4×, 12× (the wheel, a pinch or the OSD's ZOOM pill), thermal boxes on J or a long press on ZOOM. FOV 100° (`Drone.fov`; plan I19: the bench's `fpv` scene measured it at 0.79-0.93× the chase view's GPU time, under the 1.10× limit, so it stays 100°). The drone's own mesh is hidden; the shadow box reaches 50 m ahead as a ground vehicle's. The chase view sits 2.3 m behind (`CAM0.drone`). The **impact view** (`Drone.impactView`): a warhead gone off hangs the camera 12 m back and 4 m up for 1.5 s (`body.impactcam`; any key or tap ends it).
- **The OSD** (`Drone.drawOSD`, :623, main.js after `Combat.drawHUD`): white 13 px mono with a dark edge: the battery with minutes left, the link (bars, reach, JAM, LAG, FIBRE), the carrier's line, the mode (LEVEL, ACRO, HOME, LAND, LINK LOST, LAST FLIGHT) and SAFE/ARMED, a cross and a horizon, the flight line; static while the link is weak, at a launch's cut or a loss (NO SIGNAL). Its blocks are placed once a second (`placeOSD`) clear of every panel and control, and of a warning receiver's or radar's scope (`Combat.S.rwrBox`, plan I6); the words are cached in small canvases redrawn at 10 Hz: at most 40 canvas calls a frame. In the chase view the flight data gains Battery and Link rows (`droneHUD`); the `#jam-chip` shows when our jammers hold an enemy drone.
- **The alert line** (`#flight-alert`, `Flight.updateHUD`): while a bay's drone is flown `Drone.alertText` ranks first (LAST FLIGHT's count, then "YOUR TANK IS HIT · U: back" for 3 s), then the damage model's.
- **Sounds:** `Sound.buzz` (a band-passed noise loop, louder as a hostile drone closes within 250 m), `Sound.chirp` (a sensor's new contact: 900 then 1200 Hz), beeps (battery, arming), the laser's whine.
- Every control that changes how a craft flies refuses an inert one (`Flight.inert`: Home, Land, the lever).

### 5. The link, jamming and failsafe (bays section, `Drone.frame` every `LINK.DT` 0.2 s)

- **The station** (`station`): a bay drone's tank, 3 m over it (in a LAST FLIGHT, its wreck); a solo drone's take-off spot, 2 m up; an AI drone's Nest, else its spawn.
- **Quality** (`quality`, :1091) `dl.q`, the best link's: a radio or long-range link fades from 0.8 of its range to its range, ×0.35 with the ground in the way (`losK`: ten points along the line every 0.5 s); a satellite link is 1 within 50 km; a **fibre** spool replaces the controller's own radio (a fibre drone is silent and unjammable) and **snaps for good** past its length ("Fibre ran out at 5.0 km"). Each enemy jammer (`jammers`, `jamFoe`) inside its reach × the link kind's factor (`LINK.K`: radio 1, long 0.6, satellite 0.25, fibre 0) scales it by a smoothstep from 0.35 of that reach (dead) to the reach (`jam`).
- **`linkTick`**: LOST once `q` < 0.15 for 0.5 s (the stick does nothing; the controller's failsafe flies it: an FPV drops, a GPS drone flies Home); back once over 0.35 for 0.5 s; a flown bay drone is handed back to its tank after 5 s lost (it carries on by itself). A weak or satellite link brings the stick late (`Drone.lagged`, from `readControls`: up to 0.2 s, a ring of eight inputs, plus the satellite's 0.25 s).
- The JAM chip counts the enemy drones inside the jammers on the flown craft or its tank (`hud.jamN`).

### 6. Bays and a drone's life (bays section)

A drone from a bay is a vessel of its own in `F.vessels`: `v.carrier` (its tank), `v.bay` (its dock part); the tank's `C.drone` is the one it has up; `p.docked` counts what a bay has left (a bay never launched from holds its full `n`).
- **The key** (`key`, :794; U, DRONE, the pad's Tank): from a tank with a bay, take its drone again if one is up (not while its link is lost), else **launch**; from the drone, dock when over its bay, else back to the tank (`back`: the drone holds where it is; a winged one circles); in a LAST FLIGHT, end it. Tab from a bay's drone is U; the craft list skips bay drones.
- **`launch(F, C)`** (:835): the first bay with a drone left (rack, hangar, tubes). Refused, with the reason, when the tank is knocked out, tilted past 60°, in the water, moving faster than 3 m/s, every bay empty or broken, in the 2D view, or in a room while a drone of ours is up. The drone is the tank's pick (`v.droneChoice`) when it fits that bay (`pickDesign`), else the bay's stock drone (said once a tank). It hops 5 m off the deck (a tube throws a winged one out at 28 m/s, 20° up), its lid opens for 1.5 s, the tank **parks** (`Flight.switchTo(v, force, quiet)`: braked, its controls let go, its crew mending and its kit and radars at work) and the drone is flown in the view last picked for drones. A parked carrier is stepped at any distance (flight.js's near list's carrier clause, plan I14), so its kit, repairs and the AI's targeting go on 8-12 km from its drone.
- **Home and Land** (`home`, `land`, `homeSteer`, `circleSteer`): Home climbs to 30 m over the ground, flies back at up to 12 m/s facing its bay, slows and lets down over the last 15 m, and descends over the bay (moving with the tank) until it docks; a solo drone lands where it took off; a winged drone circles 60 m round its tank (or its start). Land goes straight down where it is (not a winged drone). Level or Acro takes it back.
- **Docking** (`dock`, :959; `bayFrame`): homing, landing or a GPS failsafe over its bay (1.5 m across, 2 m over, under 3 m/s against the tank), or U over it (3 m, 6 m), with the tank up: back in the bay with a fresh battery and payload, a puff, control back to the tank. A drone homing to a tank knocked out meanwhile lets down by the wreck and never docks in it.
- **Lost** (`lost`, :980; `Flight.onDestroyed`'s first line): the flown bay drone shot down, gone off, crashed or flat hands control back to its tank in the same frame, with no end screen and nothing booked (a room hears `down {c}`); its hulk falls and crashes as any (death.js); its bay doesn't get it back. Lost while nobody flew it, its tank hears it. A solo drone lost reads "Drone lost" on the strip.
- **Who removes a drone** (plan I13): `bayFrame` and `aiFrame` remove **live** drones nobody has (lost 60 s, or landed 3 s: gone with a puff, `gone`); a drone with `v.hulk != null` (down or crippled) is `Death.tick`'s alone (fall, crash, junk).
- **LAST FLIGHT** (`lastFlight`, :995; `endLastFlight`): the tank knocked out under its flown drone tells its death at once (a room: `MP.announceDown`, credit and tickets now; a Practice Battle: `GroundAI.onDestroyed(F, v, quiet)`, the wreck and tickets booked, the panel later) and the drone flies on for 20 s ("Your tank is knocked out! Last flight: 20 s", the count on the OSD, TANK KO). It ends when the 20 s run out, the drone is lost or goes off, or U; then the tank's death runs today's way (`Flight.onDestroyed`: the strip, then the end screen through `afterFall`).
- **Credit** (plan I3): `Combat.byMe(o, sim)` counts a hit by the player's drone as the player's (`o.carrier === sim.active`), so a kill finished after the drone died (a cook-off, a crew bleeding, a fire, a fall) still credits the player; GroundAI's score and KO credit use it.
- **The parked tank hit** (`alarm`, from `Combat.damage`'s one line and a room's hit): the screen's edge flashes red (`#sight-vig.alarm`), a buzz and two low beeps, the alert line and the OSD's TANK line red for 3 s.
- **Saves** (`forSave`, `Flight.serialize`): a bay's drone is never saved; its tank is, with that drone back in its bay (`GV_SAVE`'s `docked`), and is the craft flown when the save comes back. A save during a LAST FLIGHT saves nothing. No recovery with a drone up ("Bring the drone home first").

### 7. Payloads (combat section)

- **Safe-arm** (`armTick`): a warhead arms once 20 m from where it started and 2 s on (its own clock), never while it homes, lands or hops off its bay (it re-arms after); a click and ARMED on the OSD. FIRE before that says "Not armed yet: fly 20 m clear".
- **The contact fuse** (`fuse`, :1269, every substep for a drone with a warhead, not a ghost, not debris): along the way the warhead went this step and 0.3 m on (and the drone's own reach, since the vehicles' bump stops the frame first): the first vessel (never itself or its own tank, a wreck, or a picture of someone else's falling hulk), prop, or the ground or sea at over 4 m/s (slower it has landed: an FPV can sit in ambush). A step longer than the drone could fly (a launch, a restore) is ignored.
- **`detonate(v, F, at, hv, hp, why)`** (:1307): a shaped charge meeting a plated vehicle's new part is HEAT along the dive through `Armour.legacyHit`, then its burst; everything else (frag, an old craft, the ground, a prop, mid-air) is `Combat.explode`. One flash, then the drone is gone (`shatter`: no parts left; not ten explosions for a craft a hand across). The flown drone gets the impact view; one from a bay hands back to its tank. `why`: `contact`, `fire` (FIRE, armed only: an airburst where it is), `cook`.
- **Cook-off** (`cookOff`, :1344, `destroyPart` slot 3): a warhead shot to 0 hp bursts as high explosive only (no jet), 0.6 of its damage, the hitter's blast: shooting a drone near your own tank is a risk.
- **Shot down vs jammed** (plan I5): a drone whose controller is knocked out is debris, and its fuse is off: it lands without going off (the counter worked). A jammed FPV is not debris, keeps its fuse and goes off where it falls.
- **Drops and missiles**: `dropBomb` carries a grenade's or bomb's `he` (combat.js), `launchMissile` the mini missile's shaped charge (`w.heat` → `s.pen`); a loaded rack or dropper is a loaded store, so the damage model's rare detonation roll applies (deterministic dice).

### 8. Counter-drone (combat section; `Combat.S.dr` opens it)

- **Who is hostile** (`threats(o, F)`, :1360, cached per physics step; `foe`): the player's side (the player, allied AI) against AI drones not allied and, in a room, enemy players' drones; the enemy AI against the player's drones and allied AI drones; nothing against its own side or its own carrier's. A ghost's kit is its owner's to work.
- **Who sees a drone** (`marked(viewer, d)`, :1414): 1 by eye within 400 m with a clear line (`EYE`; radar.js's `eye` asks it for real drones, plan I4(b)), 2 a working detector (2 km, 4 km for a loud one) on a drone that emits (a radio link or a generator: never a fibre drone; no line needed), 3 a working Drone Radar within 4 km with a clear line (every drone, low and slow too). The sensors that count are the viewer's and, for a drone from a bay, its tank's. `seeR(v)` is a vehicle's reach for drones (the radar's, else the detector's, else the eye's); `spotR(v)` a drone camera's reach on the ground (FPV 1.5 km, gimbal 3 km).
- **Aircraft radars never see drones** (`Radar.sees`' first lines, chapter 9): the Drone Radar is a `searchRadar` with `low: true`, shown on GHUD's SPAA scope only, and with it the vehicle's radars see low, slow drones (aim.js's ground-radar clutter, `aimNotch`, never hides a drone from a vessel with `v.cdLow`).
- **Jammers**: §5.
- **The anti-drone gun** (`gunStep`, :1518, an auto-turret of its own; the old turret still parks on the ground): every 0.1 s it picks the nearest hostile drone it sees within 600 m, or 1.1 km when a sensor marks it (the cue), over its lowest elevation; leads it (the time of flight on the relative velocity, twice, with the round's drag and drop); turns and fires 10 airburst rounds a second, on the ground too. The rounds are ordinary tracers that burst 3 m from the drone in `moveShot` (`explode`'s 10th argument `fx: 'ab'`: one `nolight` flash, four sparks and a grey puff, plan I8, so ten a second stay inside the particle budget; an `explode` without it is byte for byte as before). 200 rounds.
- **The laser** (`laserStep`, :1574): the hostile drone closing fastest within 1 km (1.5 km cued), in sight with no smoke; it turns at 1.5 rad/s and, on it within 0.01 rad, dwells: the controller burns out after `0.8 + 2.2·(d / 1.5 km)²` s (`Combat.damage(d, fcPart, 1e6, 'laser', F, o)`: the air rule's controller KO, a limp hulk that falls; in a room `MP.sendHit`). Eight seconds of beam, then off until cooled to 0.3 (5 s from full). The beam is `Drone.beams` (`{b, a, e, col, w}`, at most 8), drawn by render3d's streak mesh (plan I7: no mesh, program or draw call of its own), with a `nolight` flare every 0.1 s and a whine.
- **The roof cage** is the armour's slat rule (`slat: 0.5`): half the plain shaped charges that hit it are crushed; tandem charges get through. The gun's arcs skip it as an obstacle.
- **Ghosts' kit** (`netFire`, from combat.js `netGuns` on a ghost's `pv` `gunOn`): its gun fires tracers along its aim and its laser shows its beam, for show; hits are only ever its owner's.
- **Seekers**: `candidates` adds the drones hostile to the seeker's owner, so a heat seeker or the SPAA's guns can take them; enemy proximity shells burst by the player's drones nobody flies (`ownedAir`, armour.js).
- **The marks** (`markFrame` 4 Hz, `drawMarks`, :1703): the hostile drones the flown craft sees, nearest first, at most 8: a grey ◇ by eye, an orange ◇ with its range from a detector (an arrow on the screen's edge when off it), and its closing speed when a Drone Radar holds it; a chirp for a new one, the buzz within 250 m.

### 9. The builder (builder.js)

- **The kind:** a sixth button in the kind strip (an X-frame icon; six columns) opens on the Frames tab (`KIND_TAB.drone`); picking Drone from a blueprint without drone parts starts an empty drone design (Undo brings the old one back), with a first-time toast on how six taps make one (`rss.droneTip`). The tray's groups come from `def.tray` (Controllers, Multirotor, Fixed wing, Motors, Batteries, Links, Cameras, Warheads, Drops, Missiles), appended to the radar's rule.
- **Scale:** everything scales by `Builder.unit()` (0.1 for a drone, 1 for every other kind, so they are unchanged): the view's margins and zoom, the snap radius, a 2.5 cm grid, the depth step, nudges and a ruler in centimetres. Drones mirror left and right with their own symmetry setting (`rss.symmetryDrone`).
- **A tap** (`quickAdd` → `droneSpot` → `droneSpotOf(type, parts, at)`, :544, pure, so drones.test runs it): the frame at the centre (a second refused), the controller on top, motors on the frame's arm stations front first with their mirror twins, the battery under, the link behind the controller, the FPV camera at the front edge, a gimbal under it, a charge under the battery with its tip 2 cm past the front, droppers and missiles underneath, legs flush, a guard over the nearest bare motor; a winged drone's body, pusher, wing (8 cm behind the centre of mass), tail and fin where the Lancett has them. Six taps and a charge rebuild the Bumble within 1 cm per part.
- **Stats** (`renderDroneStats`, `renderDroneLine`, `#s-link-row`): kg, thrust as times its weight, battery and hover minutes (a winged one's cruise), Level and Acro top speeds, the link, and a line (how hard it hovers, how fast it climbs, which bays it fits or why none). **Warnings** (`droneWarnings`): no controller, no motors, no battery joined, too heavy or sluggish, off balance, props lowest with no legs, a wing ahead of the balance point, armour parts aboard; grey notes for no camera and more than 40 parts. `droneLine`/`droneSpecs`: the tray's one-liners and the part card's numbers.
- **Tanks:** a Drone Rack goes on the back deck and a Roof Cage over the turret by a tap. A selected bay shows "Carries: Bumble FPV ×3 ▸" (`#pi-dock`, `renderDock`), which opens a picker (`pickDrone`: stock drones, then the hangar's drone designs; thumbnail, weight, span, price, a tick or the bay's reason; names as text). The pick is `Builder.droneChoice`, saved with the design as `drone` (only with a bay aboard, so every other design is byte for byte as before), cleaned by `Drone.cleanChoice` on load, and carried in design codes as `d` (the RSS prefix reads only the rows). The stats line ends "drones: Bumble FPV ×3"; warnings for drone parts on a tank and a pick no bay can carry. The hangar lists Drones (first while building one).

### 10. AI and the drone war (drone.js AI section, groundai.js)

- **`spawnAI(F, key, at, team, opts)`** (:1752): a stock drone for a team at a spot (`siteSpot`), or with `opts.nest` a Nest's own bay drone launched off its deck as the player's are. **`aiSteer`** (:1830), through `pre`'s autopilot target, deciding 4 times a second on the drone's own clock: climb to 30 m, cruise at 20 m/s toward the nearest enemy within 1.5 km (where it will be in 2 s), and 200 m out dive at 0.9 of its top speed onto its roof (1.5 m over its middle, led); with nobody near, over the zone nearest the enemy's pad; under 15 % charge, onto the nearest enemy anywhere. A **passive** drone (the range's practice targets) circles 60 m round its spot 50 m up at 10 m/s, never arms, and has its battery kept full. Jammed, its controller's failsafe has it. Shot down it is `Combat.aiDown`'s ("Enemy drone down!") and death.js's hulk. AI drones are never bandits (`Combat.isFighter`) and the AI loop skips them.
- **The drone war** (`GroundAI.dw`; groundai.js `DW`; never in a room, never in old play): on when the player brings a drone bay or counter-drone kit to the Tank Range (the vehicle or its carrier) or a Practice Battle (the design). Practice: two passive Bumbles circling 400 m ahead of the north pad, back 45 s after being downed, scored as the old target drones. Hostile: an attack Bumble every 60 s (two at most) from the south pad's way, always 1.6 km off (outside every counter-drone kit's reach, so the kit meets it on the way in) and at its top speed ("Enemy drone inbound!"). A Practice Battle's enemy fields a **BMK-2D Nest** in slot 1 (it launches a Bumble when it sees an enemy within 1.4 km, every 60 s, one up at a time, three a life) and the money's counter-drone vehicle in slot 2 (ZSY-23, Gepardo or MA-1C). The Hostile waves' pool leaves out the drone war's tanks (`t.dw`); a Practice respawn keeps the AI's drones; `GroundAI.reset` removes them.
- **AI counter-drone:** only anti-aircraft vehicles (`a.aa`) target drones, within their radar's 1.2 km, else their drone sensors' reach (`Drone.seeR`), so a tank's gun never stalls on one; SPAA aims at a drone's middle; a drone overhead never blocks a teammate's shot; the AI's counter-drone kit works through `Drone.step` as the player's.

### 11. Multiplayer (mp.js, gmode.js, mpui.js, ghud.js)

- **A second craft:** a player's drone from their tank's bay is their second craft in the room. Its messages carry the **craft index `c`** (1-3, appended **last**; `sendExtra` takes the next, 1 → 2 → 3 → 1); the main craft's never carry it, so every one-craft message is byte for byte as before (ground.test checks a tank with its drone up, flown from the tank or the drone). `c` rides `spawn` (kind drone, its rows), `despawn` (docked), `s` (10 a second), `pv` (a motor's throttle in tenths), `parts`, `hp`, `down`, `want`, and `shot`/`msl`/`cm` the drone fired; **`tc`** rides `msl` and `mp` when a missile is after someone's drone. `mpC(m)` refuses anything but the integers 1-3 (NaN, 0.5, '1', 4, 1e9, {}, null → −1), never taken as the main craft.
- **The owner** (`MP.extra`, one at a time): `sendExtra` on a launch, `dropExtra` on a dock (`despawn`) or a loss (`down {c, by, P}`: `x` for a warhead burst, `fall` for a drone shot down in the air, its last updates first); `stream(v, S, c, E)` streams each craft (the main craft's body unchanged); `resendSpawn(pid, c)`; `mine(c)` for a missile's `tc`; `announceDown` tells the main craft's death at once in a LAST FLIGHT (`mainDown` keeps the end screen from sending a second `down`). `startFlight`, `leaveFlight` and a respawn drop our drone. A hit on our drone lands on it after our tank is lost; each knows who hit it; the tank hit while we fly its drone raises the alarm.
- **Receivers:** `onExtraSpawn` takes a drone (at most 40 parts) only when the peer's own main craft carries a bay it fits (`Drone.fits`), one per peer, pricing and booking nothing; its record is `P.x` (c → `{c, design, ghost, snap, recv, asked, gun}`), its ghost named "‹name›'s drone" (text only). Snapshots without a record ask for its spawn (`want c`, every 3 s); silent 6 s it freezes, 15 s it goes. `onExtraDown`: `toFall(…, E)` with the record (a hulk of our physics) or a small blast; the shooter gets the toast, the chime and SHOT DOWN (`Death.feedback`, guarded); **an extra never books a death, a kill, a ticket or a feed line**. `enemyGhosts` lists drones (for seekers, turrets and counter-drone kit); `ghosts()` stays main-only; `extraGhosts()` is new; `netGuns` fires a gun key on every craft of the peer. `onLeave` drops both.
- **Seeing** (`MP.sees`, the drone lines first): a flown drone sees ground vehicles by its camera (3 km gimbal, 1.5 km FPV, 600 m with none) and so spots them for its team; a drone is seen as `Drone.marked` says. MPUI tags a peer's drone only while marked; GHUD's minimap and tactical map draw drones as small diamonds. `groundUnits` and `groundReports` count main craft only.
- **Standalone drones in ground battles:** a drone is air support (`mpAir` includes `'drone'`): offered at a respawn with a sortie point (`choices(air)`), standing where a helicopter would (`GroundMode.placement`, `padPlace`; a sandbox's helipad), and a downed one costs **10 + cost / 2,500 tickets** (`GroundMode.death(…, 'drone')`: a $1,920 Bumble 11), never the aircraft's ×1.5; every old argument prices as before. Air battles stay planes, helicopters and cars (`MP_KINDS_PVP`).
- **Protocol:** `NET_VERSION` stays 11 (the update's one bump); its comment names the drones. A peer's spawn rows go through `mpSpawnRows` as any; a forged extra spawn without a bay that fits, over 40 parts, or a second one is ignored. ground-battle.mjs measures under 20 kB a second each way with a drone up (2.7 kB/s in its run).

### 12. Stock drones and tanks (builder.js `TEMPLATES`)

After the jets: the **Bumble FPV** ($1,920: a racing quad with a shaped charge, red, radio), the **Bumble Fibre** ($2,870: on a 5 km fibre line, olive), the **Mavik Scout** ($3,190: a GPS quad with a long-range link and a zoom and thermal gimbal, 31 minutes' hover), the **Baba Yara** ($7,830: a black hex night bomber with two 3 kg bombs, 1,400 Wh) and the **Lancett** ($3,630: a winged loitering munition with a tandem charge, for Launch Tubes). After the technical: the **BMK-2D Nest** ($43,900: a BMK-2 with a rack of three Bumbles) and the **MA-1C Abrahm** ($93,926: an MA-1 with a roof cage, an anti-drone gun, a jammer and a detector on its turret, whose turret is dialled to 480 mm to carry them within its ring: the one stock design with armour dialled, so its code is RSS3). Both tanks carry `dw: true` (out of the old Hostile waves). Their baselines were additions only (fingerprint, traces, mp-measure +7; groundcombat +2).

### 13. Invariants and gotchas

1. **The drone code is shut for old designs.** A new hook opens only on kind `'drone'`, `def.drone`, `def.cuas`, `def.dock`, `searchRadar.low`, `v.carrier`, `Combat.S.dr`, `GroundAI.dw` or a message's `c`/`tc`, and `ground.test.js`'s contract (calls into `Drone`, and `Combat.S.dr`) must stay at zero.
2. **A second craft's messages carry `c`, last; the main craft's never.** Old messages stay byte for byte, and mp-air.json is identical. A new message a drone sends needs its `c` and a receiver that diverts on it first.
3. **Extras book nothing**: no death, kill, ticket or feed line for a drone from a bay; the tank's death is told by the usual path (at once in a LAST FLIGHT).
4. **One credit rule**: `Combat.byMe` (carrier-aware). Never `lastHitBy === sim.active`.
5. **Live drones are drone.js's to remove, hulks death.js's** (plan I13): skip any vessel with `hulk != null`.
6. **Never a drone to an aircraft radar** (`Radar.sees`), never a bandit (`Combat.isFighter`), never a fire (`Death.ignite`).
7. **Deterministic**: no `Math.random` in the simulation (`prand`, `dl.t`); the AI decides on the drone's clock.
8. **The HUD's budgets**: the OSD ≤ 40 canvas calls, placed clear of `Combat.S.rwrBox` and every control (read fresh each placement); a new flight-HUD control must go into `placeOSD`'s blockers. `#btn-drone` and the RDR pill never show together.
9. **Every control that moves a craft refuses an inert one** (`Flight.inert`), the drone's pad and lever too.
10. **Drone-sized**: a part's numbers are grams and centimetres; the builder's `unit()` scales everything for the kind and nothing for the others.
